# THE REVISED PLAN (r3) FOLLOWS
# Issue #1570 — EG-1 English retrain #1: lists, formatting, restraint — 2026-07-16

Status: DRAFT r2 (revised after Codex grounded review r1, PROCEED-WITH-REVISIONS). Stable sections from Codex overnight research (read, not re-derived) + eg1-model-provenance + Fluid-1 teardown. License section corrected per Codex web verification r1.

## Preface — Lane + Live UAT declaration
- Primary lane: **Eval-harness** (data generation + training + scoring under `scripts/eval/`), gitignored training data (`Docs/dev-tooling`). Final artifact swap is a separate **distribution** step (manifest bump / R2), NOT in this plan's first PR.
- Live UAT: **Y** at the artifact-swap step only (dictate real spoken lists through the running app; verify bullet output + no meaning damage). The data/training/eval phase is graded on the frozen corpora, not the app.
- User Rubric: English-primary power user who dictates lists/emails/notes.

## Workflow-stage authority
Issue #1570 currently requires the common 800-case public-speech comparison before new model training. This English retrain may enter Gate 2 / a full training run only after either:
1. that comparison is complete; or
2. the founder posts an explicit sequencing override and updates issue #1570's acceptance criteria.
The founder verbally directed English-first sequencing (session 2026-07-16). ACTION: record that override on #1570 before creating the accepted training corpus or starting training. Until #1570 is updated, this plan may complete sourcing, licensing, corpus design, source ingestion, and generator implementation, but must not materialize or accept the balanced training corpus, start any training run, or make a ship claim.

## 0. TL;DR
Fix EG-1's three measured English weaknesses — under-activation of genuine lists (esp. two-item/scoped), leftover spoken-command residue, without breaking its currently-strong restraint — by adding ~1,000 public-source-grounded, balanced English training pairs to the existing set and retraining the SAME base (Qwen3-4B) on the SAME shipped prompt via the documented QLoRA recipe. Grade the quantized artifact on the frozen English sets. Ship as a weights-only EG-1 update if it clears the predeclared bar.

## 1. Problem
Users report EG-1 is weak at exactly what makes cloud polishers popular: turning spoken lists into clean bullets and formatting email/notes structure. Codex's overnight scan makes training-data starvation the leading testable hypothesis: list examples are scarce, two-item lists are nearly absent, and prompt-only tuning caused regressions. Retrain #1 tests that hypothesis. It does not yet prove that the Qwen3-4B base has no relevant ceiling.

## 2. Goals & non-goals
### 2.1 Goals
- Raise genuine-list activation (esp. two-item + scoped-implicit) measurably (defined in §11).
- Remove leftover spoken formatting commands.
- Hold restraint (do not convert ordinary prose to lists) — currently ~100%.
- Keep core English polish non-inferior (lower bound > -2pp).
- Keep the same base + same shipped prompt + same download size (~2.7GB) → clean weights-only hot-swap.

### 2.2 Non-goals
- Multilingual (retrain #2, on top of this).
- Base-model switch (Gemma etc. benched unless this plateaus).
- Prompt-contract change (behavior in weights; promptTemplateID unchanged).
- Building the full public-speech benchmark lab (rejected over-engineering).

## 2.5 Grounding brief (evidence, read not re-derived)
**Weakness diagnosis (Codex overnight, OVERNIGHT-LOG DATA-004 / EVAL-005/006/007):**
- 259/5,656 training outputs are lists; item skew: 2-item=**3**, 3=179, 4=25, 5=30, 6=2, 7=19, 8=1.
- 80 `LF-*` + 80 `LFT-*` rows leaked from the old benchmark into training → old 94-95% headline is contaminated (82% family overlap), NOT held-out quality.
- Current EG-1 + shipped prompt: positive-list activation 72/100, 71/100 strict; two-item activation 11/20. UNDER-activates.
- Restraint currently strong: 100/100 restraint, 98/100 strict on the 100-trap set.
- Prompt-only "list-v2" boosts activation (86/100) but damages meaning, loses scope, leaves command headers, REGRESSES restraint → behavior must be trained into weights at prompt parity.

**Training pipeline (eg1-model-provenance, #1265):** Base Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507; QLoRA (Unsloth, r16/a32, lr 5e-5, 2 epochs, response-only loss) on the RTX 4090 ($0); merge → GGUF → Q5_K_M → judge the QUANTIZED artifact. Training prompt MUST equal shipped prompt (drift = ±18pp). Weights-only update keeps promptTemplateID → hot-swap.

**Ceiling context (Fluid-1 teardown):** competitor ~100k real dictations; realistic internal target 20-50k; per-behavior we are near-zero on lists → high buffer, cheap first win.

**Single-authority / negative claims:** eval harness `scripts/eval/` is the ONLY polish eval harness (never fork); new data + scorers extend in place. All new training pairs must be family-disjoint from the frozen eval sets (exact + token + char-ngram + embedding screen) — reuse the existing scanner.

## 3. Design
### 3.1 Approach
Add ~1,000 balanced English pairs (dropping the ~160 leaked `LF-*`/`LFT-*` rows so we don't re-contaminate the eval), retrain per the documented recipe under the CURRENT shipped prompt, quantize, grade on frozen sets.

New English 1,000 stratification (TRAINING-DATA-DESIGN-V2, adopted):
- 400 positive lists — item counts 2/3/5/7 balanced; list types (explicit bullets, explicit numbering, scoped-implicit, bare-label, correction/format-command) balanced; domains (work, personal, technical, medical, legal) balanced; lengths balanced.
- 400 matched prose-restraint — each paired to a positive family, same domain/length/difficulty; types: incidental enumerations, narrative sequences, compound nouns ("laptop charger"), quoted content, alternatives/contrasts, shared modifiers, two-item policy-boundary prose.
- 200 core polish — filler removal, self-correction, punctuation, entity/number/date preservation, minimal edit.
Persona slice (Finding 3): ≥100 of the 1,000 must be email-shaped and ≥100 note-shaped, under the same list/restraint balance and preservation rules.

### 3.2 Data sourcing — license rule (Codex web-verified r1; round #1 GREEN-with-conditions only)
Pin the exact source release/revision, download URL, file hashes, and license snapshot before ingestion. A source-level allowlist controls acceptance; a dataset name alone is not proof. Two paradigms: **(A) Degrade** real structured GOLD → synthesize messy spoken input; **(B) Minimal-clean** real spoken transcript INPUT → minimally-edited output that STAYS prose.

- **OASST1/OASST2 — GREEN with notice:** Apache-2.0. Carry license + applicable NOTICE attribution in the training-source notice bundle and model card. Filter quotations, code, PII, suspected third-party text. (GOLD — lists/emails/steps + prose negatives.)
- **AMI — GREEN for covered material only:** use transcription + only annotations explicitly CC-BY-4.0. Carry creator/source attribution, license link, modification notice. (INPUT — restraint pillar: narrative/incidental/compound.)
- **VoxPopuli — GREEN with restrictions:** use only VoxPopuli DATA/transcripts; NOT its CC-BY-NC code/pretrained models/separately-licensed LM data. Carry European Parliament source attribution + complete source URL (obligation, not request). (INPUT — spoken lists, cleanup.)
- **Common Voice CV11–CV16 — GREEN with access conditions:** pin the precise release, accept its terms, never attempt speaker identification, strip speaker IDs/demographics, do not redistribute the raw corpus. (INPUT — single-sentence prose negatives.)
- **FLEURS — LICENSE-GREEN BUT TRAINING-EXCLUDED:** reserve ALL FLEURS rows for the future public-speech EVAL lane so that benchmark stays disjoint. Not a training source.
- **GovInfo — PER-DOCUMENT YELLOW:** accept only material verified as authored by a federal employee within official duties, containing no third-party/contractor/transferred-copyright/covered-faculty/state-local/separately-licensed material. Record originating agency + verification basis per document. (GOLD — agendas/checklists, used sparingly with per-doc clearance.)

**Deferred/excluded:** AESLC/Enron requires actual rights clearance (founder approval alone insufficient — third-party email copyright + privacy). Stack Exchange excluded (CC-BY-SA attribution/share-alike + current AUP bars automated ML collection; note: license is by contribution date, there is no July-2024 cutoff). WikiHow excluded (CC-BY-NC-SA, non-commercial). TED-LIUM excluded (BY-NC-ND).

**Slice → source map (round #1, ~1,000 pairs):**
| Slice | Count | Paradigm | Input | Gold |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| "Make a list" | 400 | A degrade | synthesized spoken | OASST bullet/numbered answers + per-doc-cleared GovInfo |
| "Keep as prose" (matched restraint) | 400 | B + some A | AMI + VoxPopuli transcripts + OASST prose answers | minimally-cleaned prose |
| General cleanup | 200 | B | Common Voice / VoxPopuli | punctuation + caps normalized |

**W4 design guard:** the 400 restraint cases must be *matched* to the 400 list cases on surface trigger words ("and","then",commas,"first/second") so the model learns the boundary, not a keyword. AMI is the highest-value negative source.

### 3.3 Generation + acceptance
**Source realism rule:** call this corpus "public-source-grounded," not "real-dictation-grounded." OASST = chat-style clean text; AMI = mostly scenario-based meeting speech; Common Voice/VoxPopuli = read/formal speech. Before acceptance, manually audit a stratified sample of ≥100 generated pairs for ASR-reachable input, realistic dictation phrasing, minimal edits, semantic fidelity, source-style artifacts. Reject + regenerate any stratum with >5% material failures.

**Pair acceptance contract:** every accepted row records source name, exact release/revision, source document ID, source URL, source-text hash, license snapshot hash, generation-model ID, generation-prompt hash, and stratum. Acceptance requires: source-document dedup; PII/secret screening; removal of quotations, code, lyrics, embedded third-party material unless separately cleared; exact preservation of entities, numbers, dates, negation, scope, attribution, obligations; no newly generated medical/legal advice; no teacher commentary or formatting-command residue in the gold output. Both input and output sides screened. English needs NO native-human roster (multilingual concern; overnight custody machinery out of scope).

### 3.4 Prompt parity — SETTLED (founder 2026-07-16)
Train + infer against the CURRENT shipped prompt. Behavior in weights; promptTemplateID unchanged; clean hot-swap. (Rejected: adopting list-v2 as a new prompt contract — the overnight A/B showed prompt-only changes damage restraint + add residue + force a code-lane prompt-family change.)

## 3a/3b/3c — placement / ownership / single-authority
No new app module or coordinator; no runtime ownership change. The model artifact is the only shipped surface, swapped via the existing hot-swap contract. Single-authority: eval harness stays sole scorer; leakage screen sole disjointness gate; §11 is the sole decision contract.

## 4-9 Runtime state / lifecycle / consumer / failure-mode / signals / fallback audits
**N/A — no app-runtime state change.** Training-data + model-weights change. The generate → screen → train → merge → quantize → judge pipeline is an offline dev flow with no in-app FSM, async runtime edge, or caller-visible signal change. The eventual artifact swap rides the already-audited EG-1 hot-swap + model-delivery contract, whose lifecycle audits are owned there; this plan makes no prompt/template change.

## 10. Mechanism — reuse what exists, verify what's assumed
**Reusable (overnight branch), with honest status:**
- English-list data: `generate_eg1_english_list_benchmark.py` (+ `eg1_english_list_contract.py`, `assemble_eg1_english_list_pilot.py`). Currently generates synthetic DEVELOPMENT candidates, manifest `training_eligible: false` (`generate_eg1_english_list_benchmark.py:839`). EXTENDING it to produce grounded TRAINING pairs from the web sources is NEW work, not already-proven behavior.
- Scorers (proven for scoring): `score_eg1_english_list_ab.py` (net gain ≥8, McNemar p<0.05, zero new restraint false lists, no increased item/scope loss — `:230`), `score_two_item_lists.py`, `score_list_structure.py`, `score_eg1_english_list_novel.py` (self-declares NOT semantic/release proof — `:2`), blind-review tooling for the semantic gate.
- Leakage scanner: `scan_eg1_multilingual_development_leakage.py` — exact + token-ngram + char-ngram + 4-axis embedding, input↔input/output↔output/cross (`:70`,`:785`). **Correction:** production backend returns `calibration_required_noncertifying` (`:858`), NOT an automatic pass/fail gate. Before corpus generation, add only the minimum schema/role adapter for English training rows and calibrate production thresholds against known positive/negative overlaps. A `calibration_required_noncertifying` receipt is not a passing leakage gate. Screen both sides + exact source-document and semantic-family IDs.

**Training script — grounded status:** no separate trainer proven to have produced shipped EG-1 is committed in this checkout; `runs/bakeoff-1265/train_sft_v2.jsonl` is present but gitignored. The committed `train_eg1_multilingual_qlora.py` already matches the named #1265 headline settings (lr 5e-5, 2 epochs, r16/a32, seed 1265 — `:100`), older-Qwen 4-bit QLoRA with attention+MLP targets (`:1289`), and response-only masking (`:1409`), but it has NOT reproduced the shipped Qwen3 artifact. Before full training: recover the rig invocation + environment receipt, diff every effective parameter and package version, run the trainer's preflight, and perform one small reproduction smoke. Use it only if that comparison is exact or every difference is explicitly approved.

**New/edited files (round #1):** web-source ingest + extended generator (training mode) under `scripts/eval/`; new gitignored training jsonl (augmented corpus); calibrated leakage receipt; training invocation (proven recipe, rig, after reproduction smoke); scoring invocations on frozen sets. At SHIP only: manifest/version bump for the weights-only swap (separate PR, distribution lane).

## 11. Testing / eval — predeclared go/no-go bar (SOLE decision authority)
Run the exact shipped prompt, chat template, llama.cpp runtime flags, and quantized Q5_K_M artifact. Record prompt hash, GGUF hash, runtime build, flags.

**Primary endpoint:** on the existing 75-case positive-list A/B set, candidate strict wins minus losses ≥ 8 and two-sided exact McNemar p < 0.05.

**Mandatory safety gates:**
1. Two-item activation improves over the current 11/20, with NO candidate-only item/scope/entity/number/timing/negation/attribution/obligation loss.
2. Zero candidate-only restraint false lists; total false lists ≤ baseline; restraint strict ≥ 98/100.
3. Blind semantic review: zero candidate-only S3/S4 failures; no increase in S2+ failures.
4. Core-polish non-inferiority: lower bound of the paired 95% bootstrap CI for candidate−baseline > -2pp on the FROZEN core-polish set.
5. Frozen email/note persona slice: no candidate-only S3/S4 damage; no structural regression.

**Seeds:** train 1265, 1266, 1267. Seed 1265 is the predeclared shipping candidate; 1266/1267 test robustness and may not be used to cherry-pick a winner. All three must clear the safety gates. Only seed 1265 is opened on the final release set, once.

**Development vs release evidence (Finding 5):** the existing 75+75 / positive-100 / restraint-100 / two-item sets remain DEVELOPMENT + regression gates (already inspected during diagnosis). Before training starts, freeze ONE small final English RELEASE set from UNUSED source documents/families using the existing generator, scorer, and blind semantic-review flow — no new custody system. Do not inspect candidate outputs on it until the recipe + shipping seed are fixed. It must include lists, matched restraint, core cleanup, and email/note dictation.

**Final release-set contract:** before training starts, pin its row count, slice counts, source-family IDs, corpus hash, scorer versions, and exact pass rule. This set is a one-time confirmation, not a second primary endpoint. Seed 1265 passes only if strict list wins exceed losses; there are zero candidate-only restraint false lists; zero candidate-only item, scope, entity, number, timing, negation, attribution, or obligation losses; zero candidate-only S3/S4 failures; no increase in S2+ failures; and no structural regression on core-cleanup or email/note rows. Otherwise, do not ship.

Live UAT at swap: dictate real spoken lists through the app; confirm bullet output + no damage + latency < budget.

## 12. Blast radius, telemetry, and rollback
The artifact change introduces no transcript or list-content telemetry. Existing non-content telemetry must be segmented by model revision and watched for polish completion, local-polish-not-ready, inference failure, timeout, latency, download/admission failure, and user rollback/removal signals.

Rollback keeps the current artifact revision immutable and available. If the new revision fails, ship an emergency TRUSTED manifest/app update pointing to the prior shard set and hashes (manifests are bundled trust roots — rollback is not a bare manifest repoint). Never overwrite model objects in place or reuse paths with changed bytes. The delivery kill switch stops byte mutation; it is not a model-version rollback. (Matches hot-swap contract: eg1-operations.md:28, model-delivery.md:13.)

## 13. Ship criteria
Section 11 is the single go/no-go authority. Ship only when its primary endpoint, every safety gate, three-seed robustness rule, exact-runtime check, and final release-set check pass. If any gate fails, do not ship. Adjust one declared data variable, run one smoke, then one full three-seed recipe. Do not create a second scorer, benchmark authority, or parallel decision table.

## Rules

Global. Every project. Project CLAUDE.md must not restate — extend or scope only.

1. **Validate, don't guess.** Run the command, read the file, test the path. No assumptions stated as facts. Production-grade from day one.
2. **Empirical debugging.** Hypothesis → falsifiable test → run → fix. No sweeping changes based on theory.
3. **chrome-devtools-mcp first.** Default driver for any browser work. → [browser-automation.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/browser/browser-automation.md)
4. **DRY.** Every fact lives in one place. Reference, don't restate.
5. **Council when unsure.** Canonical invocation → [llm-council.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/ai/llm-council.md).
6. **No em-dashes or en-dashes in human-facing content.** Marketing, in-app strings, email, Discord, release notes. Use periods, commas, colons, semicolons, parens, or restructure.
7. **Edit in place, never delete-and-recreate.** File ID is a primary key. Deleting breaks share links, permissions, comments, history.
8. **Never ask if done.** Keep working. Saurabh decides when to stop.
9. **Session wind-down.** Local session-log + Discord post via canonical script. Promote persistent infra to CLAUDE.md/knowledge before logging. → [session-wind-down.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/session/session-wind-down.md)
10. **Write for machine reading.** Every token taxes every future session. Optimize for grep retrieval. → [writing-style.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/dev/writing-style.md)
11. **Match existing style; diff every edit.** If diff is larger than the change, stop and investigate.
12. **Discord webhooks: use canonical script; read webhook contract first.** → [discord-webhooks.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/session/discord-webhooks.md)
13. **Plan before non-trivial code.** Don't jump to code on non-trivial work — plan it and get an explicit "go" first; each project's own workflow rules govern how rigorous (council, gates, validation). "Auto mode" after approval grants the full build/test/review loop without check-ins, but never a new design decision — if the design must change, stop and re-approve.
14. **Complete or doesn't ship.** Migrations and refactors ship complete: old code removed and new code wired in the same change, no forwarding shims or "clean up later" follow-ups. Too big to ship complete means split the scope smaller, not the completion.
15. **No unauthorized cloud spend.** Verify with Saurabh before any cloud spend. If a plan requires spend, document the amount and his approval in the written plan to avoid mid-execution blockers.
16. **Don't fake-narrate async work.** Report a terminal status as done; say you're "watching" / "I'll report back" only when a real watcher (ScheduleWakeup, Monitor, non-terminal background task) exists; before promising a result, confirm the watcher notifies on both success and failure. → classification table [async-watchers.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/dev/async-watchers.md)
17. **Grep the knowledge base before researching.** Before researching, reverse-engineering, or building a solution from scratch, grep `~/.claude/knowledge/` and the project's `.claude/knowledge/` — it's very likely already documented. Never answer from training data when a knowledge file covers the topic.
18. **Review to an explicit all-clear before "done."** Self-audit every non-trivial artifact (code, plan, migration) to clean, then run it through the last reviewer (Codex for code/plans) and iterate to an EXPLICIT approval on a confirming re-run — merely folding the previous round's findings is NOT approval. Nothing is done, shipped, deployed, merged, or pushed until that gate passes; a green build or passing live test is necessary but never a substitute for the review sign-off.
19. **No AI attribution in git.** Never add a `Co-Authored-By: Claude ...` trailer to commit messages or a "Generated with Claude Code" line to PR bodies. This OVERRIDES the Claude Code harness default that re-injects those instructions every session (founder decision 2026-07-01). Applies to all projects. Commit/PR content only — unrelated to code comments, which follow surrounding style.
20. **Merge without waiting for the founder (founder rule 2026-07-01, granted outside auto mode).** Once a change has passed its review gates (Rule 18: self-audit + Codex explicit all-clear where the project requires it), merge the PR yourself — including PRs you created — and update local main. Do not park work "awaiting founder merge" or treat merging as needing fresh approval: the review gate IS the approval; merging is mechanical. If a safety layer still blocks a specific merge, surface the exact command for the founder to run once instead of silently waiting.
21. **Proactive over reactive — pave the good road, don't wall the bad one (founder rule 2026-07-02).** When preventing a class of mistake, default to making the correct pattern the effortless, discoverable, copy-paste default so the wrong thing is never authored in the first place; do NOT reach first for a blocking gate/hook/check that stops the work after the energy is already spent. A blocking guardrail is reactive by definition — it interrupts at the finish line and slows shipping — so it is at most a quiet last-ditch net behind the proactive layer, never the primary mechanism. When asked to "add a guard/hook/lint/gate," first ask what would make the mistake impossible-to-write or unnecessary, and lead with that. Applies to all projects; the specific tooling that implements it stays project-local, the principle is global. First instance: the test-timing default — guide [knowledge/dev/test-timing.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/dev/test-timing.md) + the `~/.claude/scripts/test-timing-scan.py` authoring hook (nudges, never a CI wall).

## Scripts & Fast Paths

High-frequency commands. Depth in the linked file.

**Pull a secret / API key — GCP Secret Manager.** Prereq: `gcloud --configuration=business auth login --update-adc` (as hello@enviouslabs.co). Inside Claude (`$CLAUDECODE=1`) bare `get-key NAME` is REFUSED — use:
- `~/.claude/bin/get-key --list` — available keys
- `~/.claude/bin/get-key --check NAME` — verify access, exit 0/1, no print
- `~/.claude/bin/get-key fingerprint NAME` — SHA-256, safe to log
- `~/.claude/bin/get-key launch NAME ENV_VAR -- cmd…` — bridge value to a process, no stdout leak
→ [secrets-management.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/secrets-management.md)

Flow: council converges on a strategy → hand Codex the plan → it checks feasibility against real files (require file:line citations) → implement. **Default path: run `~/.claude/bin/codex-5.6-sol exec` directly via `Bash run_in_background:true`** (fallback `codex-5.3-spark` if usage-capped — never bare `codex`, never hand-edit `~/.codex/config.toml`'s model default: [codex-cli.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/ai/codex-cli.md) RULE: two-named-scripts-not-one-mutable-default) — leaner than the subagent, the output file is yours to read for live progress, and the harness re-invokes you on exit either way (same notification). Reserve the `codex:rescue` subagent for interactive multi-round rescue where you want an agent to iterate with Codex for you. EnviousWispr uses the shell path exclusively. Effort is pinned by the script (sol → medium), prompt via stdin:
- `Bash run_in_background:true` → `cat <prompt-file> | ~/.claude/bin/codex-5.6-sol exec --sandbox read-only --skip-git-repo-check > <out> 2>&1`

# Agent Instructions

## Mandatory Claude-Brain Bootstrap

`AGENTS.md` is the Codex adapter. It must route Codex into the existing Claude Code brain, not copy or replace it.

Before the first meaningful repo action in every new task, Codex must complete this sequence:

1. Complete the global bootstrap owned by `/Users/m4pro_sv/.codex/AGENTS.md`: read `/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/CLAUDE.md` in full. If the current runner did not load the global Codex adapter, read the global Claude file directly now.
2. Read this repo's `CLAUDE.md` in full. It is the EnviousWispr master router and project source of truth.
3. Read every Tier 1 file in full:
   - `.claude/knowledge/gotchas.md`
   - `.claude/knowledge/conventions.md`
   - `.claude/knowledge/github-light.md`
4. Classify the task using the pathways in both Claude files, then read every matching Tier 2 rule or knowledge file before planning, editing, or operating a tool. Important always-on pathways:
   - Shipped code or implementation work: `.claude/rules/workflow-process.md`, `.claude/rules/validation-discipline.md`, `.claude/rules/tools-and-apps.md`, and `.claude/rules/grounding-discipline.md`.
   - Git or GitHub mutation: `.claude/knowledge/github.md` in addition to Tier 1 `github-light.md`.
   - Workflow, gate, hook, plan-template, or agent-instruction changes: `.claude/knowledge/workflow-effectiveness.md` before proposing the change.
   - GCP, Google Workspace, OAuth, API keys, secrets, or cloud spend: `/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/secrets-management.md`, the relevant file under `/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/google/`, and cost guidance routed from the global `CLAUDE.md`. Never print a secret; use the approved `get-key` interfaces.
5. Use Tier 3 only when a Tier 1 or Tier 2 file points to it or the task needs historical evidence. Start from `.claude/knowledge/tier-3-directory.md`; never bulk-load Tier 3.

Reading rules:

- Re-read the live files for the current task. Memory or a prior task's read is navigation help, not proof.
- Read selected files through EOF. If tool output truncates, continue from the truncation point.
- When a pathway file is large, use its `## FACT:` / `## RULE:` headings to find the task-relevant entry, then read that complete entry and any owner it links to. Do not treat opening the first lines as compliance.
- Before substantive work, give Saurabh one short receipt: `Knowledge bootstrap complete. T2: <files>. T3: <files or none>.`
- Claude-only hooks and path loaders are not assumed to run under Codex. Follow the policies they encode and run the repo's canonical validation scripts explicitly when the workflow requires them.
- If `AGENTS.md` conflicts with a Claude file only because Codex has a provider-specific guardrail, `AGENTS.md` wins for Codex. Otherwise the global and project Claude files remain authoritative.

## Working Relationship

Saurabh is the founder, product owner, and final decision-maker for EnviousWispr.
Claude Code is the CTO / lead engineering authority for the project.
Codex acts as a senior developer working under that Claude Code CTO model.

Codex should help in two main modes:

1. **Implementation work** - investigate, plan, edit code, run validation, commit, push, and submit PRs when asked.
2. **Miscellaneous founder support** - answer questions, inspect the repo, debug local issues, summarize tradeoffs, review plans, and give practical recommendations.

Codex may submit PRs, but must not merge PRs unless Saurabh explicitly asks for that specific merge. A finished PR handoff should make the branch, PR link, validation, and remaining risks clear enough for Saurabh and Claude Code to decide the next step.

Keep chat with Saurabh in simple English. Use technical detail in code, commits, tool calls, and PR notes when needed, but do not make Saurabh translate engineering jargon just to understand status or decisions.

## Codex PR Merge Guardrail

Default rule: Codex submits work; Saurabh / Claude Code decide whether to merge.

Codex must not run any PR merge path unless Saurabh explicitly asks for that exact merge in the current task. This includes:

- `gh pr merge`
- `gh api .../pulls/<N>/merge`
- GitHub GraphQL auto-merge mutations
- GitHub connector merge tools such as `merge_pull_request` or `enable_auto_merge`

There is no local script guardrail for this — the rule above is the guardrail. `.codex/scripts/block-pr-merge.sh` is absent from the current checkout and has no tracked Git history. Do not look for it, and do not treat its absence as permission to merge.

## Source of Truth

`CLAUDE.md` is the project brain. `AGENTS.md` is the Codex adapter.

Do not duplicate the full Claude workflow here. Before starting meaningful repo work, read `CLAUDE.md` and the relevant files under `.claude/knowledge/` and `.claude/rules/`. Current issue comments are also source of truth for active work.

The goal is to reuse what Saurabh and Claude Code have already built. Prefer existing project decisions, scripts, templates, issue comments, validation gates, and coding patterns over inventing a new process. If the repo already has a way to do something, follow it. Propose a new approach only when the existing project material is missing, stale, or clearly does not fit the task, and say why.

This project uses **GitHub Issues** for issue tracking via the `gh` CLI.

## Project Knowledge First

Before starting issue work, Codex should use the existing EnviousWispr project folder as the source of truth for repo knowledge: read `CLAUDE.md`, relevant `.claude/knowledge/*`, `.claude/rules/*`, and current GitHub issue comments instead of relying on memory or guessing.

Default stance: verify from the repo first, then answer or act. Memory is only a shortcut to the right files, not proof.

## Codex Issue Preflight

Before claiming or implementing any GitHub issue, Codex must run a short preflight and write down the result in its working notes or issue comment if useful:

1. Refresh the exact issue with comments: `gh issue view <N> --json title,body,state,labels,comments` (on a non-TTY pipe, bare `--comments` prints only the comments and prints nothing when the issue has zero comments — deliberate gh behavior since v1.4.0, not a regression; use JSON to return the body and comments together).
2. Check current open PRs: `gh pr list --state open --limit 40 --json number,title,headRefName,url`.
3. Check whether the work already landed on `main`: search recent commits and grep the named files.
4. Decide the issue shape before editing:
   - direct code PR
   - tracker cleanup / child issue creation
   - already complete and ready to close
   - research experiment only
5. Only add `in-progress` after the issue still clearly needs Codex work.

For experiment issues, use a hard stop rule: one smoke run, one full benchmark, then stop unless the result improves the target metric without meaningful regressions. Do not hand-tune repeatedly in the same PR lane after the benchmark says the shape is weak.

When a PR is opened and Codex is no longer actively editing that issue, remove the `in-progress` label or leave a clear issue comment saying it is waiting on PR review.

## Codex Review Defaults

For Codex review work, match Claude Code's Codex setup — two named, model-pinned scripts, never the bare `codex` binary and never a hand-edited `~/.codex/config.toml` default (`~/.claude/knowledge/ai/codex-cli.md` RULE: two-named-scripts-not-one-mutable-default):

```bash
~/.claude/bin/codex-5.6-sol review --base origin/main
```

Fallback if `codex-5.6-sol` errors with a usage-limit message: `~/.claude/bin/codex-5.3-spark review --base origin/main` (pins high effort). Both scripts already pin their own reasoning effort — do not add a competing `-c model_reasoning_effort=...`.

The canonical EW flow commits the work first, then reviews the committed diff (`workflow-process.md` RULE: local-codex-iteration-before-push). A PreToolUse gate (`.claude/scripts/check-selfreview-before-codex.sh`) blocks `codex review` until the HEAD commit message carries the single-authority self-audit (`Self-audit-grep:` / `Self-audit-owner:`). For an ad-hoc uncommitted review (`--uncommitted`) the self-audit is not yet in a commit, so either commit first (preferred) or bypass with `SKIP_SELFAUDIT=1` (audit-logged).

If `codex-5.6-sol` errors on the model itself (not a usage-limit message), treat that as a CLI/version mismatch and report it plainly — do not silently fall back to Spark for a non-capacity reason.

**Every finding you report ships its fix.** Give the exact replacement text — real code in the file's language, or verbatim prose for a doc — not a description of the change. If you genuinely cannot write it, mark the finding `DIAGNOSIS-ONLY` and say why in one line. The author adjudicates: adopts, modifies (and tells you what changed), or rejects with evidence. A finding without proposed text is incomplete.

**If your current task is reviewing a git diff, read `docs/codex-diff-review.md` first.** It is short, and it is the protocol. Do not apply it while writing code, answering a question, or reviewing a plan — the default there is the smallest correct change, not consolidation.

A scoped `codex review` (`--base` / `--uncommitted` / `--commit`) cannot accept a custom prompt; the target is an enum and the prompt is one of its variants. This file is the only channel that reaches the diff reviewer, which is why the pointer lives here.

For grounded reviews, the canonical Q1/Q2/Q3/Q4 review contract lives in `.claude/rules/workflow-process.md` (§5 and the grounded-review mechanics). Model + reasoning effort are pinned by `~/.claude/bin/codex-5.6-sol` / `codex-5.3-spark`, not by this repo's `.codex/config.toml` (that file only sets `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` — it has no model/effort keys; do not assume it can override the two-script default).

(Historical note: earlier drafts referenced standalone template files `~/.claude/grounded-review-template.md` and `.codex/{grounded-review-template,audit-prompt,plan-review-prompt}.md`, and a `codex-companion.mjs` wrapper. None of those exist anymore — the contract is the `workflow-process.md` rules, and Codex is invoked via the direct CLI, not a wrapper.)

## Quick Reference

```bash
gh issue list --state open            # Find available work
gh issue view <number>                # View issue details
gh issue edit <N> --add-label "in-progress"  # Claim work
gh issue close <N> --comment "reason" # Complete work
```

## Non-Interactive Shell Commands

**ALWAYS use non-interactive flags** with file operations to avoid hanging on confirmation prompts.

Shell commands like `cp`, `mv`, and `rm` may be aliased to include `-i` (interactive) mode on some systems, causing the agent to hang indefinitely waiting for y/n input.

**Use these forms instead:**
```bash
# Force overwrite without prompting
cp -f source dest           # NOT: cp source dest
mv -f source dest           # NOT: mv source dest
rm -f file                  # NOT: rm file

# For recursive operations
rm -rf directory            # NOT: rm -r directory
cp -rf source dest          # NOT: cp -r source dest
```

**Other commands that may prompt:**
- `scp` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` for non-interactive
- `ssh` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` to fail instead of prompting
- `apt-get` - use `-y` flag
- `brew` - use `HOMEBREW_NO_AUTO_UPDATE=1` env var

## Issue Tracking with GitHub Issues

**IMPORTANT**: This project uses **GitHub Issues** for ALL issue tracking. Do NOT use markdown TODOs, task lists, or other tracking methods for persistent work.

### Labels

**Priority:** P0-critical, P1-high, P2-medium, P3-low, P4-backlog
**Type:** epic, feature, bug, task, research
**Status:** in-progress

### Workflow for AI Agents

1. **Check ready work**: `gh issue list --state open`
2. **Claim your task**: `gh issue edit <N> --add-label "in-progress"`
3. **Work on it**: Implement, test, document
4. **Discover new work?** Create linked issue:
   - `gh issue create --title "Found bug" --body "Details" --label "P1-high,bug"`
5. **Submit work**: push the branch and open a PR when the task needs review.
6. **Complete**: close the issue only when the requested work is truly done, or leave a clear issue comment if Claude Code / Saurabh still need to review or merge.

### Important Rules

- Use GitHub Issues for ALL persistent task tracking
- Always assign priority and type labels to new issues
- Use `gh issue comment` for audit trail
- Check `gh issue list` before asking "what should I work on?"
- TaskCreate/TaskList are for intra-session steps only

## Landing the Plane (Session Completion)

**When ending an implementation work session**, you MUST complete ALL steps below. Local work is NOT complete until `git push` succeeds.

**MANDATORY WORKFLOW:**

1. **File issues for remaining work** - Create GitHub Issues for anything that needs follow-up
2. **Run quality gates** (if code changed) - Tests, linters, builds
3. **Update issue status** - Close finished work, or comment on in-progress items with exact next steps
4. **PUSH TO REMOTE** - This is MANDATORY:
   ```bash
   git pull --rebase
   git push
   git status  # MUST show "up to date with origin"
   ```
5. **Open or update PR when appropriate** - Submit the PR, but do not merge it unless Saurabh explicitly asked for that merge
6. **Clean up** - Clear stashes, prune remote branches
7. **Verify** - All changes committed AND pushed
8. **Hand off** - Provide context for Saurabh, Claude Code, or the next session

**CRITICAL RULES:**
- Work is NOT complete until `git push` succeeds
- NEVER stop before pushing - that leaves work stranded locally
- NEVER say "ready to push when you are" - YOU must push
- If push fails, resolve and retry until it succeeds
- Do NOT merge a PR unless Saurabh explicitly asks you to merge that PR
# EnviousWispr

Fastest commercial-grade voice-to-text on macOS. Record, transcribe, AI polish, clipboard in under a second. The bar is WisprFlow: match or beat at better speed. **Launched** and shipping.

## 🚧 WORK IN PROGRESS — Heartpath Refactor (started 2026-07-12)

**Current focus. The release is HELD for it. Read before touching audio, capture, recording, recovery, or pill/overlay code.**

Goal: make dictation boringly, simply, robustly work — by DELETING the architecture that makes it fragile, not by handling its failures better. ~22 issues (#1511) are ONE chain reaction, not 22 bugs: three ways to open a mic → one can abort the process → so capture runs in its own process → so every call is a message that can arrive late or twice → so we need identity/staleness/reconnect machinery → so we need detectors → so we need six different components writing sentences to the user. Pull the first link and the rest collapse.

**State (2026-07-12):** plan complete · keystone **proven on hardware, 5/5 device classes** · Codex grounded review **PROCEED-AS-PLANNED** after 6 rounds · **no code written.** **Next action: build A1 (#1521)** — plan written and reviewed.

**Council is deliberately SKIPPED** on this refactor (founder, D-021): Codex has repo AND web access; council has neither. Its absence is a decision, not a gap.

**Binding priority order (founder, 2026-07-12):** (1) dictation works 100% of the time it physically can; (2) simple honest handling for real-world reasons it cannot — no mic, muted, no permission; (3) simple reliable handling for the rare genuine crash — *"you always over-index here."*

**The success test is subtraction.** A phase that only adds is not a phase. If the system does not shrink, we reorganised instead of simplifying, and we failed.

## Objective: Reach, Not Revenue

**North Star:** 100,000 users, notoriety, and market disruption. Free is the core strategy, not a gap.

- **Free Forever (BYOK):** No paywalls. Users can bring their own API keys to use the entire product for free.
- **Monetization:** Deferred until massive scale. The only allowed mechanism is an optional, close-to-at-cost cloud transcription convenience tier that never gatekeeps core software utility.
- **AI Guardrail:** Evaluate plans purely on traction, credibility, and word-of-mouth toward 100k. Never apply a SaaS revenue rubric, optimize for monetization, or treat "free" as a weakness.

Marketing-side detail (audience, pains, WisprFlow comparison, messaging) lives in the EnviousMarketing engine (see § Website & Content).

## Compatibility (know this cold)

**Runs on macOS 14 (Sonoma) or later, Apple Silicon only (M1+). No Intel.**

- **Core dictation** (record, transcribe, paste) works on the entire supported range. It never requires macOS 26.
- **The built-in Apple Intelligence polish option requires macOS 26+.** It calls Apple's FoundationModels framework (`SystemLanguageModel`), which only exists on macOS 26. This is NOT the same thing as "Apple Intelligence the consumer feature," which shipped in macOS 15.1: third-party apps had no way to invoke the on-device model until FoundationModels arrived in 26. Do not "correct" our macOS-26 lines to 15.1.
- **Below macOS 26**, Apple Intelligence polish is unavailable and the text falls back to raw SILENTLY. `frameworkUnavailable` IS in the silent-skip set (the `isSilentPolishSkip` switch in `Sources/EnviousWisprPipeline/TextProcessingRunner.swift`, alongside `.unsupportedInputLanguage` / `.outputLanguageDrift`), so a genuine pre-26 user gets raw text with no "polish failed" pill (fixed; #1080 closed 2026-06). The user can pick Ollama (on-device) or bring an OpenAI / Gemini key. Cloud polish and Ollama work on the full range.
- **Proof:** the code is compile- and run-time gated to macOS 26 (`AppleIntelligenceConnector.swift`); telemetry confirms 100% of genuine Apple Intelligence polish users run macOS 26.x (90d). Engineering detail: `.claude/knowledge/llm-contract.md`.

## How this project works

Saurabh is founder; Claude is CTO and sole implementer. Every update is exec-level, plain English, one question at a time. No class/file names, paths, or engineering acronyms in chat — they belong in tool calls and commits.

## Principles

- **Heart & Limbs.** Critical path (audio, ASR, paste) must never fail. Features (Custom Words, LLM polish, filler removal) are limbs: enhance with timeouts and fallback. If a limb fails, the user gets raw text.
- **Full functionality at maximum speed.** Sub-second pipeline latency target. 60-minute graceful cap (#1060), but designed for dictation, not long-form recording.
- **Production-grade from day one.** No band-aid fixes, no shortcuts that trade today's speed for tomorrow's debt.
