# Gotchas & Non-Obvious Patterns
## FACT: pathway-loaded-gotchas
| Scope | File |
|---|---|
| Swift compiler patterns (FluidAudio qualification, `@preconcurrency`, CFString literals, `nonisolated(unsafe)`) | `.claude/rules/swift-patterns.md` |
| Audio / streaming / AVAudioEngine / VAD / overlay | [`gotchas-audio.md`](gotchas-audio.md) |
| Release / signing / notarization / CI / Sparkle | [`gotchas-release.md`](gotchas-release.md) |
| LLM polish contract invariants | [`llm-contract.md`](llm-contract.md) |
| WhisperKit-specific traps | [`whisperkit-research.md`](whisperkit-research.md) § Non-Obvious Traps |
| Incident response runbooks | [`incident-response.md`](incident-response.md) |
## RULE: carbon-hotkey-after-nsapp-run
Rule: Register Carbon `RegisterEventHotKey` only from `applicationDidFinishLaunching` or later, never before.
Why: Earlier registration returns `noErr` (success) but events are silently never delivered.
Ref: `AppLifecycleCoordinator.runDidFinishLaunching()` → `DictationRuntime.startHotkeyServiceIfEnabled()` → `HotkeyController.startIfEnabled()`.
## RULE: cgevent-needs-axtrust
Rule: CGEvent-based paste needs Accessibility trust to deliver. The shipped path (`PasteService.dispatchCmdV()`) posts Cmd+V via a `.combinedSessionState` source through `.cgAnnotatedSessionEventTap`; `pasteToActiveApp()` reads `AXIsProcessTrusted()` for telemetry, NOT as a per-call gate — the app-level mandatory-Accessibility onboarding (#735/#736) is what guarantees delivery.
Why: On macOS 14+, posted events silently never deliver without Accessibility, regardless of tap level.
Ref: `PasteService.pasteToActiveApp()` / `PasteService.dispatchCmdV()`.
## RULE: modifier-only-needs-accessibility
Rule: Any modifier-only hotkey default MUST be paired with Accessibility-mandatory onboarding (#735); chord defaults via Carbon do not need this.
Why: Global flags-changed monitors silently fail outside the app without Accessibility; Carbon chord paths do not.
Ref: PR #736.
## RULE: permission-status-prompt-only
Rule: Treat `permission.status=granted` for Accessibility with care; `dictation.completed` is the cleanest proxy for "ended up granted".
Why: TWO emit paths now — `requestAccessibilityAccess()` (`PermissionsService.swift`) on explicit prompt, AND `refreshAccessibilityStatus()` (`PermissionsService.swift`, granted in `refreshAccessibilityStatus()`) with `context:"changed"` when the background poll detects a System-Settings grant. So `permission.status=granted` is no longer purely explicit-prompt-only, and Settings grants are no longer invisible.
Ref: 2026-05-15 #733 funnel analysis.
## RULE: activate-ignoring-other-apps-dead
Rule: Use `PasteService.forceActivateApp(pid:)` (sets `"AXFrontmost"` as CFString via AX API) for Tier 2 paste-back activation; do not use `.activateIgnoringOtherApps`.
Why: macOS 14+ ignores the `activateIgnoringOtherApps` flag for LSUIElement apps, breaking paste-back entirely.
Ref: `PasteService.forceActivateApp(pid:)`.
## RULE: audio-constants-from-architecture
Rule: Use `architecture.md` Audio Constants for the canonical capture format, tap sizing, VAD chunking, and pre-roll policy; call `SilenceDetector.reset()` (resets stream state to `.initial()`) before every new session.
Why: Stale VAD state corrupts the next recording.
Ref: [architecture.md](architecture.md) Audio Constants (canonical values); runtime VAD thresholds + filtering + state machine behavior live in [pipeline-mechanics.md](pipeline-mechanics.md) `vad-config-and-filtering`.
## RULE: keychain-two-backends
Rule: Release builds use Apple Keychain (`com.enviouswispr.app.api-keys` service); DEBUG/dev/unrecognized bundles use `~/.enviouswispr-keys/` (0700 dir, 0600 files); never UserDefaults, never log keys, never delete files in `~/.enviouswispr-keys/` (rotate by overwriting).
Why: Wrong storage leaks keys; deletion breaks app and MCP connectors that share the directory.
Ref: `KeychainManager`.
## RULE: ollama-silent-failure
Rule: Before debugging polish code, check Ollama binary existence, then reachability at `http://localhost:11434`, then model availability.
Why: When `ollama serve` is down, polish silently fails under the strict readiness timeout.
Ref: `OllamaSetupService`.
## RULE: tccutil-bundle-id-scope
Rule: Always scope `tccutil reset` to a bundle ID — `com.enviouswispr.app` for prod, `com.enviouswispr.app.dev` for dev; never blanket-reset Accessibility.
Why: Bare `tccutil reset Accessibility` wipes permissions for ALL apps system-wide, breaking window managers, automation tools, screen readers.
How to apply: If TCC stops persisting, verify cert (`security find-identity -v -p codesigning | grep "EnviousWispr Dev"`) and bundle ID (`plutil -extract CFBundleIdentifier raw "build/EnviousWispr Local.app/Contents/Info.plist"`); on dev+prod conflict, quit both, reset both bundle IDs, reboot, re-grant production first then drag dev `.app` into System Settings.
Ref: [distribution.md](distribution.md) § Dev Certificate.
## RULE: uat-runner-background-only
Rule: Run `python3 Tests/RuntimeUAT/uat_runner.py run` with `run_in_background: true`; retrieve via `TaskOutput`.
Why: Foreground UAT silently fails (listing via `uat_runner.py list` is fine in foreground).
Ref: `Tests/RuntimeUAT/uat_runner.py`.
## RULE: asr-backend-unload-before-swap
Rule: Only one ASR backend is active at a time; always unload the active backend → swap → warm the new backend (`ASRManager.switchBackend(to:)` unloads+swaps, then `EngineCoordinator.startWarm(for:)` / `ensureEngineWarm(reason: .engineSwap)` warms it).
Why: Two active backends corrupt lifecycle ownership.
Ref: `ASRManager.switchBackend(to:)`; `EngineCoordinator.startWarm(for:)`.
## RULE: ui-state-via-named-homes
Rule: Route UI-facing pipeline state via `LiveRecordingState.pipelineState` / `LiveRecordingState.currentTranscript`, post-recording error via `LastRecordingResult.polishError`, and reset via `DictationRuntime.resetActivePipeline()`; never reach into a concrete pipeline's `.state` / `.lastPolishError` / `.reset()`.
Why: Direct pipeline access bypasses WhisperKit when it is the active backend.
Ref: 2026-03-22 audit Q2/Q20.
## RULE: accessibility-monitoring
Rule: `PermissionsService.startMonitoring()` polls Accessibility using `TimingConstants.accessibilityPollIntervalSec` (`Constants.swift`) and calls `refreshAccessibilityStatus()` to catch runtime revocation; `resetAccessibilityWarningDismissal()` re-arms the warning banner. Note: `AppLifecycleCoordinator.runDidBecomeActive()` does NOT refresh on activate — the background poll is the live mechanism.
Why: Users can revoke Accessibility at runtime.
Ref: `PermissionsService.startMonitoring()` (`PermissionsService.swift`).
## RULE: nsscreen-guard-first
Rule: Guard `NSScreen.screens.first` with `guard let` or `??`; never force-index `NSScreen.screens[0]`.
Why: Display sleep/wake, monitor disconnect/reconnect, and window-server transitions return an empty `NSScreen.screens` array — `[0]` crashes with index-out-of-bounds.
Ref: `NSScreen.screens`.
## RULE: no-cp-into-worktree
Rule: Apply deltas to a worktree via `git diff -- <file>` + `git apply`; never `cp` files from the root checkout into a worktree.
Why: Root may contain unstaged changes from prior sessions; copying silently includes them in the worktree branch commit.
Ref: PR #109 CI failure.
## RULE: foundationmodels-build-gate
Rule: The required PR gate `build-check` must depend on a `macos-26` lane (`latest-stable` Xcode) that compile-probes FoundationModels; post-#906 that lane is `build-release` in `pr-check.yml` (standalone probe, `build-check` aggregates both lanes). `main-post-merge.yml` has an SDK-version guard (`macOS SDK 26.0+ required`) + Release build, but NOT a standalone `#if canImport(FoundationModels)` compile probe.
Why: `#if canImport(FoundationModels)` is compile-time — missing SDK silently strips AI code and ships unexplained polish failure; after the #906 split `build-check` is an aggregator, so the probe lives on the release lane, not the gate job itself.
Ref: PR #712 (2026-05-09); #906 (2026-05-29).
## RULE: xcode-test-entrypoint
Rule: Run logic tests through `scripts/xcode-test.sh`, never raw `swift test`. The wrapper drives `xcodebuild test` against the generated Tuist project (`-project EnviousWispr.xcodeproj -scheme EnviousWispr -configuration Debug`, arm64) with a "Test run with N test" executed-count guard; `--release` opts into the full release-config test RUN locally (mirrors `main-post-merge.yml`, Release config, `ENABLE_TESTABILITY=YES`) — useful for reproduction, though the PR `build-check` gate itself now compiles (not runs) both Release test targets on its own (#1539). Still wrap `#if DEBUG`-gated symbols (`TelemetryService.testEventHook`, `CapturedTelemetryEvent`) in `#if DEBUG` in the test file.
Why: Raw `swift test` no longer proves the shipped app/XPC/resource topology; release-lane runs catch `#if DEBUG`-symbol compile errors.
Enforced (nudge, not a block): `.claude/scripts/check-swift-build-gate.sh` (PreToolUse Bash) fires on raw `swift test` or `swift build -c release` and points here; conventions.md RULE: xcode-build-engine-cli owns the enforcement detail.
Ref: #913 PR7; [ci-pipeline.md](ci-pipeline.md). (was: never-raw-swift-test, PR #610.)
## RULE: xcode-test-script-path-must-match-worktree
Rule: Invoke `scripts/xcode-test.sh` using the SAME worktree's own path you're testing (relative `scripts/xcode-test.sh` from inside that worktree, or that worktree's absolute path) — never the root checkout's absolute path from inside a feature worktree.
Why: The script derives `PROJECT_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)"` from its OWN invocation path (`$0`), not `cwd` (`scripts/xcode-test.sh:19`). Invoking root's absolute script path from a worktree silently `cd`s into and tests ROOT's checkout instead — full "TEST SUCCEEDED" output, plausible test count, zero of your new tests actually run, no error of any kind.
How to apply: From a worktree, run `./scripts/xcode-test.sh` (relative) or the worktree's own absolute path; if unsure which ran, `grep -c "<your new test name>" <log>` — a genuine 0 with "TEST SUCCEEDED" means it tested the wrong checkout, not that your test was skipped.
Ref: #1392/#1393 (2026-07-11); distinct from tools-and-apps.md RULE: claude-scripts-absolute-path-from-worktrees (that one covers gitignored `.claude/scripts/` returning a silent no-op; this one covers tracked `scripts/` silently testing the wrong checkout).
## RULE: xcode-rebuild-relaunch
Rule: After any change needing runtime validation, run `/wispr-rebuild-and-relaunch` (→ `scripts/build-dev-app.sh`: quit ALL running dev instances by `EnviousWispr Local.app` executable path → Tuist generate → xcodebuild the `EnviousWispr-Dev` scheme → self-signed → copy to `build/EnviousWispr Local.app` → relaunch → verify by worktree path); `/wispr-run-smoke-test` is compile-only.
Why: Users validate the bundled `build/EnviousWispr Local.app`, not a raw `swift build` binary. Only one dev EW runs at a time (founder 2026-07-01). Dev and prod share the `EnviousWispr` executable name, so cleanup is scoped to `EnviousWispr Local.app` paths — never `pkill -x EnviousWispr`, never a bundle-ID quit (the `.dev` id is shared across worktrees).
Ref: #913 PR4/PR7. (was: stale-binary-rebuild-relaunch, PR #109.)
## RULE: xcode-build-after-commit-for-push-gate
Rule: When a ship-path (`Sources/`/`Tests/`/`Package.swift`/`Project.swift`/`Tuist.swift`) commit must pass the push-freshness gate (`.claude/scripts/check-push-discipline.sh`: newest of `build/EnviousWispr Local.app/Contents/MacOS/EnviousWispr` and `.derivedData/Dev/Build/Products/Dev/EnviousWispr Local.app/Contents/MacOS/EnviousWispr` executable mtime ≥ HEAD commit epoch), run `scripts/build-dev-app.sh` AFTER committing — building first then committing leaves the executable older than the commit and the gate blocks the push.
Why: The gate keys freshness to the Xcode dev-build outputs (PR4), not a SwiftPM `.build` binary. Never `SKIP_PUSH_CHECKS=1` to dodge it (banned guardrail bypass).
How to apply: `git commit --amend` (the standard post-Codex-review fix) bumps the committer epoch past your last build and re-blocks the push. A plain rebuild does NOT fix it when no Swift source changed: the content-aware build system skips the relink so the artifact mtime stays stale, and `touch`ing a source with unchanged content does NOT force a recompile. Force a genuine relink by `rm -rf` the built `.app` (BOTH the DerivedData product `.derivedData/Dev/Build/Products/Dev/EnviousWispr Local.app` AND the `build/` copy), then re-run `scripts/build-dev-app.sh` (object files stay cached → fast relink, not a full recompile). When checking freshness by hand, stat the bundle EXECUTABLE (`Contents/MacOS/EnviousWispr`), not the `.app` dir — the dir mtime stays stale across incremental builds and will fool you.
Ref: 2026-05-30 PR #908; #913 PR4/PR7; 2026-06-07 PR #1016 (amend/relink trap re-confirmed). (was: build-after-commit-for-push-gate; spm-never-rm-build-dir deleted — `.build` is no longer the canonical build dir.)
<!-- tier: 1 -->
# Engineering Conventions
Production-breaking rules live in [gotchas.md](gotchas.md); workflow policy lives in [`.claude/rules/workflow-process.md`](../rules/workflow-process.md).
## RULE: xcode-build-engine-cli
Rule: Build EnviousWispr with Xcode's build engine driven from the CLI via the committed Tuist manifests (`Project.swift` / `Tuist.swift`). Do not commit the generated `.xcodeproj` / `.xcworkspace`, and do not treat raw `swift build` / `swift test` as the build or test path for the app, XPC services, release archive, or repo test gate.
Why: The SwiftPM-only path could not place Core ML / SwiftPM resources where the generated `Bundle.module` accessor resolves them in a signed `.app` (#913). Xcode owns app + 2 XPC + test + resource assembly; Tuist keeps it reproducible and CLI-only with no committed project files. (`swift build` of the package products still compiles as an informal quick sanity check, but it is not the canonical build and never produces the shippable bundle.)
How to apply: Dev bundle → `scripts/build-dev-app.sh` (Tuist generate → xcodebuild `EnviousWispr-Dev`/`Dev` config → `build/EnviousWispr Local.app`); logic tests → `scripts/xcode-test.sh`; release DMG → `scripts/build-release-dmg.sh <version>`. Kill old dev processes by `EnviousWispr Local.app` executable path (ALL dev instances — one dev EW at a time, founder 2026-07-01), never `pkill -x EnviousWispr` (dev and prod share the `EnviousWispr` executable name). Enforced (nudge, not a block): `.claude/scripts/check-swift-build-gate.sh` (PreToolUse Bash) fires on raw `swift test` or `swift build -c release`/`--configuration release` and points at the real script; plain `swift build` (debug, with or without `--target`) stays silent as the allowed sanity check. The real wall is downstream: `check-push-discipline.sh` keys push freshness to the Xcode-engine artifact's mtime, so a raw swift build/test can never silently satisfy Definition of Done even if the nudge is ignored.
Ref: [xcode-build-tooling.md](xcode-build-tooling.md); [gotchas.md](gotchas.md) RULE: xcode-test-entrypoint; 2026-07-11 (this session caught itself using `swift build -c release` as the release gate, no hook existed).
## FACT: commit-style
Conventional commits: `type(scope): message`, squash-merged. Types: `feat` / `fix` / `refactor` / `docs` / `chore` / `test` / `perf`. Scopes: `asr` / `audio` / `ui` / `llm` / `pipeline` / `settings` / `hotkey` / `vad` / `build` / `user` / `release`. Example: `feat(hotkey): chord default for fresh installs`.
## RULE: di-narrow-homes
Rule: Inject the narrow `@Environment` home a view needs (e.g. `LiveRecordingState`); never add a broad cross-domain state object and read it as `<object>.<domain>...`.
Why: That god-object pattern is what made AppState the deletion target of epic #763.
How to apply: Settings changes route through `PipelineSettingsSync.handleSettingChanged()`; AppState is freeze-test blocked from reintroduction; full decision tree in [state-ownership.md](state-ownership.md).
Ref: epic #763.
## RULE: settings-persistence
Rule: Persist non-sensitive settings via `UserDefaults.standard` with `didSet`; persist API keys via `KeychainManager` (Apple Keychain for release `com.enviouswispr.app`, file storage at `~/.enviouswispr-keys/` for DEBUG/dev/unrecognized bundles, failing closed).
Why: Failing closed means UAT and beta builds never touch the production Keychain.
Ref: [gotchas.md](gotchas.md) RULE: keychain-two-backends.
## FACT: view-patterns
- Settings tab body: `SettingsContentView { ... }` (branded ScrollView wrapper in `SettingsComponents.swift`; replaced `Form { }.formStyle(.grouped)`).
- Main window: one unified window — `UnifiedWindowView` (`SettingsView.swift`), a `NavigationStack` + custom `HStack` whose sidebar is a `ScrollView` of button rows (NOT a `NavigationSplitView`/`List` — that was replaced for macOS-26 sizing; the sidebar renders as `AXButton`s, uat-testing.md #1296) driven by the `SettingsSection` enum (History + every settings tab); onboarding is a separate `Window(id: "onboarding")`.
- Async on-appear work: `.task { }`.
## FACT: release-versioning
Semver tags with `v` prefix (`v1.2.3`) trigger release CI; local builds carry `-local` suffix (`0.0.0-local`). Full release flow: [github.md](github.md) RULE: release-workflow and [distribution.md](distribution.md). Changelog: `/wispr-generate-changelog`.
## RULE: brand-visual-system
Rule: Use the brand system at `.claude/skills/brand-guide/SKILL.md` for all visual artifacts (HTML mockups, diagrams, landing pages) — tokens, fonts, gradients.
Why: It is the SoT; ad-hoc choices drift the brand.
How to apply: macOS settings use system-ui fonts plus brand accent purple (`#7c3aed`) instead of the default blue.
Ref: `.claude/skills/brand-guide/SKILL.md`.
## RULE: feature-request-docs
Rule: Track feature specs as GitHub Issues; save plans at `docs/feature-requests/issue-<N>-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<slug>.md` (no-issue plans: `plan-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<slug>.md`); all plans outside the zero-blast-radius exception use `TEMPLATE.md` with §4-9 mandatory regardless of tier.
Why: Bias toward SMALL tier is a documented failure mode; legacy files stay to avoid history churn.
How to apply: Commit scope matches the primary code area (e.g. `feat(hotkey):`). New naming applies from 2026-04-14 forward.
Ref: [workflow-process.md](../rules/workflow-process.md) RULE: ten-step-shape (step 3).
## RULE: logging-convention
Rule: Use `AppLogger.shared` actor (always `await` at call site) via `log(_:level:category:)`; never log secrets.
Why: Single actor avoids duplicate sink setup; secrets in logs leak in CI artifacts.
How to apply: Levels `.verbose` / `.debug` / `.info`; categories `"Pipeline"` / `"LLM"` / `"Audio"` / `"PipelineTiming"`; the ENTIRE log pipeline (OSLog + file sink) is `#if DEBUG`-only — release builds are dead code, no output; in DEBUG, OSLog receives every call and the file sink at `~/Library/Logs/EnviousWispr/app.log` (5×10MB rotation) is active only when debug mode is enabled; `CORRECTION_DEBUG` category traces RAW ASR → WordCorrector → Filler Removal → LLM Polish per utterance.
Ref: PR #85.
## RULE: xpc-error-sanitize
Rule: Never call `safeReply(error as NSError)` in `ASRServiceHandler` (the sole remaining XPC service handler after #1543 collapsed audio capture in-process); always pass error paths through `XPCErrorSanitizer.sanitizeForXPC(error)` with the `// XPC error sanitization boundary.` anchor comment.
Why: Unsanitized NSError userInfo can SIGABRT the helper during XPC encode.
How to apply: `scripts/check-xpc-error-hygiene.sh` enforces this in CI.
Ref: issue #297.
## FACT: runtime-cross-refs
Runtime behavior, types, data flow: [pipeline-mechanics.md](pipeline-mechanics.md) (text processing, streaming, audio capture, paste cascade) and [architecture.md](architecture.md) (state machines, modules, window architecture).
## RULE: no-dotclaude-from-shipped-code
Rule: Never reference `.claude/` from shipped files — `Sources/**`, `Tests/**`, `Package.swift`, `.github/workflows/**` (comments / docs / runtime strings), `website/src/**`, `website/public/**`, `workers/**` (prompts shipped to the cloud); inline the salient sentence, move load-bearing content to `docs/` and link there, or delete the cross-ref if it is just attribution.
Why: Ship-path `.claude/` links break on GitHub, fresh clones, and cloud worker prompts.
How to apply: Allowed exceptions are runtime calls to `~/.claude/bin/get-key`, CI path-allowlist regex patterns, and content explicitly about the `.claude/` folder (e.g. blog posts).
Ref: PR #717 (2026-05-10).
<!-- tier: 1 -->
# GitHub — Everyday Essentials (auto-loaded subset)
Daily-work subset: finding issues, running PRs, and the git-safety systems that stop wrong commands. Full reference (branch protection detail, CI internals, releases, dependabot, codeowners, runner migration, SPM cache): [github.md](github.md) — read on demand before release / CI / workflow work.

## FACT: lane-first-question
The first question for any edit: tracked in git, or gitignored? Verify in 5s — `git check-ignore -v <path>`: nothing returned = tracked = full GitHub process; a match = gitignored = direct edit, no PR possible (that is the `Docs/dev-tooling` lane, not a "Local" lane — no such lane exists).

Lane names, globs, obligations, artifacts: `phase3-validation.md` FACT: lane-obligations (sole owner). When Codex is still required on gitignored work: `workflow-process.md` FACT: lane-classification. Neither is restated here.

## FACT: finding-issues
Read an issue WITH its comments — always `--json`, never bare `--comments`. On a non-TTY pipe `--comments` prints ONLY the comments (it drops the body and metadata) and prints nothing at all when the issue has zero comments, so it can read as an empty success. This is deliberate `gh` behavior since v1.4.0 (cli/cli#2462), not a bug and not a 2.92.0 regression — a cli/cli maintainer confirmed this on issue #13452 (the one we filed). The `--json` form returns body + comments together regardless of TTY:
`gh issue view <N> --json title,body,state,labels,comments`
| Action | Command |
|---|---|
| List open work | `gh issue list --state open` |
| List active | `gh issue list --label "in-progress"` |
| List epics | `gh issue list --label "epic" --state open` |
| Issues in epic | `gh issue list --milestone "Smart Polish v2"` |
| Claim | `gh issue edit <N> --add-label "in-progress"` |
| Comment | `gh issue comment <N> --body "..."` |
| Close | `gh issue close <N> --comment "Reason: what was done, PR #, validation status"` |
| Create | `gh issue create --title "..." --body "..." --label "P2-medium,feature" --milestone "Epic Name"` |

Every issue needs a priority label (P0-critical … P4-backlog) and a type label (epic/feature/bug/task/research). Children of an epic get the epic's milestone; standalone issues get none. GitHub Issues are the persistent tracker; TaskCreate/TaskList are intra-session only.

## RULE: pr-workflow
Rule: Feature branch from `origin/main` (worktree if concurrent sessions); implement + commit (conventional commits); push; open PR; clear the cloud-review gate; THEN arm `--auto`; watch to terminal state; verify main green.
How to apply: Do NOT arm the merge at open. First clear the cloud-review gate (full mechanics: github.md RULE: codex-cloud-review-gate) — post `@codex review`, run `poll-codex-review.sh <pr> <head_sha>` in `run_in_background: true`, evaluate findings, fix anything real in-PR. THEN `gh pr merge <pr> --squash --auto --delete-branch`; watch via absolute path `/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr/.claude/scripts/poll-pr.sh <pr>` in `run_in_background: true` (`.claude/` gitignored — relative path silently no-ops from worktrees). Push with `git -C <worktree-path> push ...`. If main post-merge fails, the most recently merged code-PR session owns the repair.
Ref: PR #596; cloud-review gate 2026-06-22.

## RULE: phase-pr-closes-keyword
Rule: A PR shipping ONE phase of a multi-phase issue uses `Part of #N` / `Updates #N`, never `Closes #N Phase 1`.
Why: GitHub closes greedily on bare `Closes #N` and ignores trailing qualifiers, erasing the audit trail for remaining phases.
How to apply: Only the final phase's PR uses `Closes #N`. If auto-closed by mistake: `gh issue reopen <N>` + status comment.
Ref: #847.

## FACT: branch-protection
`build-check` is the ONLY required check on every PR; it aggregates `build-debug` and `build-release` (debug + release build + test-target compile; the FoundationModels compile probe runs on `build-release`, not on the aggregator job itself). `polish-quality-gate` advisory. Strict/up-to-date is OFF, so a moved main needs no rebase. Force-pushes and branch deletion blocked. All human changes to `main` go through PRs; only the release bot pushes directly.

## FACT: pr-merge-delete-branch-worktree-noise
`gh pr merge <pr> --squash --auto --delete-branch` run from a FEATURE WORKTREE prints `failed to run git: fatal: 'main' is already used by worktree at <root>` — this is BENIGN, NOT a failed merge. The merge AND the remote-branch delete succeed; only gh's LOCAL post-merge branch-delete fails (it tries to switch the local checkout to `main`, which the root worktree holds). Verify with `gh pr view <pr> --json state` (expect `MERGED`); the later `git push origin --delete <branch>` returns `remote ref does not exist` (already gone). Do NOT retry the merge or assume failure. Ref: #1200 (2026-06-23).

## FACT: push-discipline-hook-two-dot-diff-false-positive
`check-push-discipline.sh`'s build-freshness check computes `CHANGED_FILES` via a TWO-DOT diff (`git diff --name-only "$BASE_REF"..HEAD`), not the merge-base-aware three-dot form (`BASE_REF...HEAD`). If your worktree branched off an OLDER `origin/main` commit and `main` has since advanced with unrelated Swift changes, the two-dot diff attributes THOSE files to your branch too — the hook then denies the push with "touches ship-path files... run scripts/build-dev-app.sh," even when your actual diff (`git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD`, three-dot) never touched `Sources/`/`Tests/`/`Package.swift` at all. This is the SAME two-dot-vs-three-dot bug class the CI classifier (`scripts/ci/classify-changes.sh`) was already fixed for (`ci-pipeline.md` FACT: detect-code-changes-extracted-to-script) — the push-discipline hook was not.
Why matters: branch-protection's "Strict/up-to-date is OFF, so a moved main needs no rebase" (FACT: branch-protection above) is true for GitHub's own merge requirements, but does NOT mean your LOCAL push is immune to this local hook's false positive — a stale worktree base can still block your push on phantom Swift changes.
How to apply: Before troubleshooting a "no local dev build found" denial on a diff you're confident is Swift-free, run `git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD` (three-dot) yourself to check for real ship-path files. If it's clean but the hook still denies, `git fetch origin && git rebase origin/main` in the worktree (re-run tests after, a rebase replays commits) — this makes `BASE_REF` and your branch's actual base converge, which fixes the two-dot diff's symptom without touching the hook. Do not reach for `SKIP_PUSH_CHECKS=1` as the first move; the rebase is the real fix and is good git hygiene regardless.
Ref: #1433 (2026-07-09), PR #1437 — a Worker-lane-only diff got denied because main had advanced with unrelated `PasteCascadeExecutor.swift` changes since the worktree was created.

## RULE: git-safety-systems
Rule: Enforce `.claude/rules/session-behavior.md` RULE: root-stays-on-main, which solely owns root placement and worktree policy.
How to apply: `command-safety.sh` denies checkout/switch resolving to root; `check-protected-paths.sh` denies ship-path edits in root while on main; `check-commit-gate.sh` blocks commit forms that bypass the staged-file ship-path check. Check `pgrep -f claude` before any operation touching root HEAD. When Claude cannot perform a required root correction, the founder runs it via the `!` prefix (that path is not hooked). Never use `--no-verify`, admin bypass, or `--no-gpg-sign` to get past a block — fix the cause.
Ref: `command-safety.sh`, `check-commit-gate.sh`.
# Workflow Process

How code moves from idea to merged. Council checks coverage; Codex checks plan facts and the diff against code reality.

## RULE: never-batch-deny-prone-commands
Rule: Run deny-prone commands (`gh issue view`, protected-path edits, `git push`, anything a PreToolUse hook can deny) alone or first in a turn. A deny cancels the parallel batch; follow its text before batching safe work.
Ref: 2026-05-30 #832/#913; `.claude/scripts/gate-gh-issue-view.sh`.

## RULE: main-writes-code-subagents-readonly
Rule: The main thread writes all code. Subagents may scan, research, perform persona council, or review read-only; never delegate implementation.
Ref: pre-#154 incidents.

## RULE: ten-step-shape
Rule: Every shipped-product code change follows these 10 ordered steps. Council output feeds Grounded Review, not a separate revision pass. After every PROCEED-WITH-REVISIONS, revise and repeat Grounded Review until PROCEED-AS-PLANNED.

- **0. Gate 0 — Prior context.** Run `gh issue view <N> --json title,body,state,labels,comments` AND `grep -nE "#<N>([^0-9]|$)" .claude/knowledge/session-log.md`; post one paragraph: `Prior context for #<N>: ...`. If an adopted plan exists, skip to Gate 2. Use `--json`, never bare `--comments` (github-light.md FACT: finding-issues). `check-issue-prior-context.sh` partially enforces new-plan creation; `gate-gh-issue-view.sh` enforces the command shape.
- **1. Gate 0.5 — User Rubric.** For epics #314 / #316 / #317 / #320 / #321, answer `TEMPLATE.md`’s 9 questions for a named `personas.md` persona; paste all 9 answers, or `User Rubric: N/A — internal-only`, before Gate 1. Enforced structurally by `check-rubric-answered.sh`.
- **2. Gate 1 — Intent check.** Report user benefit in persona words, UX, value, and preliminary scope. If adding responsibility to a coordinator/manager/App-owned home or introducing one, state: `This will live on [X] because [reason]; the alternative would be [Y] but [trade-off].` Skip that sentence only for prompt/copy/catalog/config. **STOP for approval.**
- **3. Plan — Grounding brief → Design → self-audit → Council.** Save `TEMPLATE.md` as `docs/feature-requests/issue-<N>-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<slug>.md`. Complete §2.5 before §3. Trace producer → owner → consumer end to end, naming each concrete mechanism. Paste code evidence for every symbol, module edge, process/init claim, quoted literal, negative claim, and owner search. Complete §3b Active-epic cross-check after evidence establishes current topology. Before design, run a problem-only Codex consult when the decision depends on topology/who-calls-whom, async/XPC/cancellation/state-machine lifecycle, dependency lifecycle machinery, an unsettled negative infrastructure claim, or an uncertain owner search. Ask for current reality and naive-design traps, not a solution. `check-knowledge-prereads.sh` non-blockingly supplies matching FACT/RULE headings as `additionalContext` and audits read/unread status. Before step 4 and every step-5 round, re-walk §2.5 evidence, sanity-check scope counts, state `self-audit clean` in chat, and revise if false. Council runs only after this sequence.
- **4. Council — ONE round, coverage only.** Ask: `What did we forget? Premise, placement, metric, workflow-stage, validation-strategy, persona.` Run GPT + Gemini with §2 inline preamble; use persona-roleplay for human-centric features. Save raw outputs to `/tmp/<scope>/{gpt,gemini}-critique.md`. Council does not certify code claims.
- **5. Grounded Review — iterate to PROCEED-AS-PLANNED.** Run Codex (`codex exec`) read-only. If the plan changes module boundaries, ownership, orchestration, feature placement, dependency direction, or cross-module visibility, instruct Codex to read `.claude/rules/architecture-rules.md` before Q1; its laws are vetoes, not a mandate to invent a deeper layer. **Q1 Direction:** validate structural choice or pivot. **Q2 Fact-check:** classify every council finding TRUE / FALSE / PARTIAL / JUDGMENT-CALL with grep-verified `file:line`. **Q3 Consolidation scan:** lead with dominant root concern yes/no/uncertain, confidence, recommended single owner/tool, consolidation sites, and independent concerns that must not merge. `No consolidation` is valid; correctness/security findings remain mandatory. **Q4 Proposed edits:** every finding includes concise, verbatim, drop-in replacement text; owner: RULE: codex-findings-ship-fixes-and-i-adjudicate. Verdicts: PROCEED-AS-PLANNED / PROCEED-WITH-REVISIONS / PIVOT. PROCEED-WITH-REVISIONS mandates step 6 and another step 5. Only PROCEED-AS-PLANNED unlocks Gate 2. PIVOT means re-plan. Same-root recurrence across ≥2 rounds means STOP and consolidate to one authority before another round. Backstop: flat/rising unresolved root clusters after 4 rounds means escalate to the founder with a forced choice: consolidate / split PR / rewrite plan / accept-with-documented-rationale / revert partial fixes and restart from a clean plan / declare N independent concerns. For cross-product/FSM surfaces use RULE: matrix-review-for-cross-product-surfaces.
- **6. Revise — one pass per round.** Apply the Codex verdict and accepted council findings in one edit. PIVOT supersedes the original plan. After PROCEED-WITH-REVISIONS, rerun step 5 unconditionally and recheck old plus new defects. Never return to council. Stop only on PROCEED-AS-PLANNED.
- **7. Gate 2 — Plan sign-off.** Present the revised plan in plain English with blast radius and council/Codex digest. Precondition: step 5 returned PROCEED-AS-PLANNED. Any other verdict at Gate 2 is a process violation. **STOP for approval.**
- **8. Build.**
- **9. Phase 3 — validation.** Run `scripts/validate-pr.sh` → `.validation/runs/<timestamp>-<shortsha>/`. Required caller order: logic tests → Codex code-diff, iterated to clean → smoke + Live UAT using ONE rebuild on clean code, never between Codex rounds (RULE: codex-clean-gates-runtime-uat) → push → open PR → evaluate GitHub Codex cloud review before arming auto-merge (local clean ≠ cloud review; github.md RULE: codex-cloud-review-gate) → arm `--auto`. Before push, record any skipped step in `skip-note.txt` or escalate to Stage 2. Smoke is not UAT. Mechanics and lanes: [phase3-validation.md](../knowledge/phase3-validation.md). Note: the current `validate-pr.sh` scaffolder performs smoke before writing its Codex stub; reconcile that script before claiming it enforces this order.

Ref: #329.

## RULE: plain-english-human-gates
Rule: At Gate 0, Gate 0.5, Gate 1, Gate 2, mid-task pivots, and wind-down, use no class/file names, API jargon, or bare tier labels. Write so a non-coder can catch a wrong direction.
Ref: project communication baseline.

## RULE: gate-0-adopted-plan-shortcut
Rule: If Gate 0 finds an adopted plan, read it, summarize `Plan from <date>: <one paragraph>. Intent still matches?`, and skip to Gate 2. Do not rewrite it or rerun council.
Ref: `check-issue-prior-context.sh`; Gate 0 hook.

## FACT: lane-classification
Six exact-case lanes, enforced by `validate-pr.sh`: `Code`, `Content`, `CI/workflow`, `Eval-harness`, `Worker`, `Docs/dev-tooling`. There is no `Local`. Gitignored `.claude/`, `docs/`, `CLAUDE.md`, and root `scripts/*.sh` are `Docs/dev-tooling`. Path globs, obligations, and artifacts are owned solely by phase3-validation.md FACT: lane-obligations.

Primary signal: `git check-ignore -v <path>`; a match means gitignored and no PR is possible.

Codex still applies to gitignored work. Logic-bearing `Docs/dev-tooling` changes, including hooks, validators, workflow scripts, lane/rule/plan logic, command recipes, topology-critical docs, or future-agent decision paths, require a `codex exec` grounded check. Prose-only typo/session-log/narrative edits do not. See validation-discipline.md RULE: codex-grounds-topology-claims.

All `website/**` is Content lane, including `website/src/{layouts,pages,utils}`. Logic may raise tier and validation, never lane. Multi-lane PRs use one primary lane plus `mixed_pr: true` per phase3-validation.md FACT: lane-obligations, never `Mixed`. Verify tier; do not assume SMALL.
Ref: PR #370; Gemma4 2026-04-14.

## RULE: council-skip-zero-blast-radius
Rule: Skip plan council only when ALL hold: size ≤20 lines and ≤3 files; no new symbols/types/protocols/public APIs, control-flow change, module dependency, migration/schema/persistence change; surface is exactly one model-catalog row / LLM prompt string / user-facing copy / single-value config or constant / version or appcast bump; single-commit revert needs no downstream cleanup. Any failure or ambiguity requires full process. PR tag: `council-skip: zero-blast-radius (<bucket>)`. Still require 1 Codex diff round, 1 human sanity-check, and tier-appropriate tests/rebuild.
Ref: #272.

## RULE: council-skip-codex-grounded-review-settled
Rule: Skip council only for pure-architecture refactors (`User Rubric: N/A`) when RULE: grounded-review-mechanics is clean and remaining forks are mechanical: target name, observability annotation, capture shape, or type rename. Add `council-skip: codex-grounded-review settled the only structural fork, no user surface, no design ambiguity remaining`.
How to apply: Council remains mandatory if the plan changes a workflow gate, plan-template field, validation lane, hook, memory rule, future-plan review mechanism, CI workflow structure (jobs, required checks, runner labels), a numerical target with two readings, or a fitness gate without earliest-failure-point specification. This skip is not zero-blast-radius. User-facing epics #314 / #316 / #317 / #320 / #321 always require persona-roleplay council.
Ref: Phase E #502.

## RULE: council-coverage-only
Rule: Council, GPT + Gemini via `council`, reviews coverage gaps: premise, placement, metric definition, workflow-stage placement, validation strategy, persona perspectives, telemetry, onboarding, and customer communications. It never validates code claims, `file:line`, or API surfaces; Codex grounded review owns those.

- API-detail dispute: stop, add a temporary diagnostic log, rebuild, test empirically, remove it.
- Preamble and persona prompt: [`council-operations.md`](../knowledge/council-operations.md).
- Invocation, flags, retention, rollback: [`~/.claude/knowledge/ai/llm-council.md`](file:///Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/ai/llm-council.md).
- User-facing epic roleplay: 7 personas × 2 providers = 14 background invocations.
- Relay: orchestrator → main reads verbatim via `council search` / `tail-session`; main → user is plain-English synthesis.

Ref: #219.

## RULE: codex-modes-not-interchangeable
Rule: `/codex:review` and `/codex:adversarial-review` review existing code, uncommitted diff, or branch-vs-base. `/codex:rescue` delegates investigation or fixes. Never interchange them. Both read the local working tree; no GitHub round-trip is required.
Ref: PR #265.

## RULE: local-codex-iteration-before-push
Rule: Keep code-review iterations local; do not push until Codex returns clean. Loop: (1) `scripts/xcode-test.sh` green and each fix checked with fast inline `swift`, no rebuild; (2) commit; (3) `codex review`; (4) amend findings; (5) repeat 3-4 until clean. Then follow RULE: codex-clean-gates-runtime-uat → step 9 → [`github.md`](../knowledge/github.md). An early PR for reviewer discussion may use follow-up commits, never force-pushed amends.
Ref: PR #273.

## RULE: codex-clean-gates-runtime-uat
Rule: Reach a fully clean Codex code-diff pass before rebuild + Live UAT. Never rebuild/relaunch/UAT between Codex rounds. Order: code → self-review → logic tests and Codex cycles to clean → ONE rebuild + relaunch + Live UAT. A runtime defect means fix → Codex clean again → rebuild + re-UAT.
How to apply: Between Codex rounds, use fast inline `swift`, amend, and re-review. A second rebuild is allowed only for a genuine runtime-only defect after its fix reconverges through Codex. Live UAT remains mandatory and final per validation-discipline.md `runtime-uat-catches-static-misses`. See [phase3-validation.md](../knowledge/phase3-validation.md) FACT: phase3-sequence.
Ref: founder directive 2026-06-20.

## RULE: codex-invocation-and-effort
Rule: Use `~/.claude/bin/codex-5.6-sol` primary, or `~/.claude/bin/codex-5.3-spark` only after `codex-5.6-sol` reports a usage limit, inside `Bash run_in_background: true`; never bare `codex`. Use `codex-5.6-sol exec` with stdin and `--sandbox read-only` for grounded review/consult; use `codex-5.6-sol review --base origin/main` for diff review. Scripts pin effort: sol medium, spark high. For hard diagnosis/root cause, add `-c model_reasoning_effort=high` to sol, or named-in-chat `=xhigh` to spark; sol also accepts `xhigh`.
How to apply: `codex review` accepts either `--base` / `--uncommitted` / `--commit` or custom `[PROMPT]`, never both. Run scoped review bare. Root `AGENTS.md` is its only steering channel; owner: `~/.claude/knowledge/ai/codex-cli.md` FACT: review-target-is-an-enum-agents-md-is-the-only-prompt-channel. Do not wrap in a general Agent; `codex-rescue` exists. `codex-5.6-sol` requires codex-cli >= 0.144.0; on the newer-version error run `npm install -g @openai/codex@latest`.
Ref: 2026-05-04; 2026-07-02; 2026-07-09; `~/.claude/knowledge/ai/codex-cli.md` RULE: two-named-scripts-not-one-mutable-default.

## RULE: codex-findings-ship-fixes-and-i-adjudicate
Rule: Every Codex finding must include a fix: exact bash/Swift for code or verbatim prose for docs, never diagnosis alone. This covers `codex exec` review/consult, `codex review`, `/codex:rescue`, and confirm/receipt rounds. Prompts require: `Q4 (mandatory): exact bash/Swift for code findings, verbatim prose for doc findings; a finding without text is incomplete.` I adjudicate each finding: adopt, modify, or reject with repo evidence. Never blind-paste, silently re-derive, or omit deviations, including one-line lint changes, from the next prompt.
How to apply: Run adopted fixes and verify with [[measure-with-the-real-tool-never-a-simulation]]. Scoped `codex review` cannot take a prompt; root `AGENTS.md` → `docs/codex-diff-review.md` carries the demand. Council is exempt because it lacks repo access; see [[dont-council-our-own-agentic-architecture]].
Ref: #1457; #1462; founder 2026-07-10.

## RULE: self-review-and-grep-before-codex
Rule: Before any `codex review` or `codex exec`, self-review to an honest all-clear. Re-read the diff or plan for scope drift, naming/whitespace inconsistencies, debug residue, incomplete renames, and premise mismatch. Before committing any renamed/added/bumped/signature-changed identifier, including values, constants, events, telemetry keys, functions, CLI flags, or public APIs, run `grep -rn` for OLD and NEW across `Sources/ Tests/ docs/ .claude/ workers/ website/ scripts/`.

Also run the single-authority audit before Codex: Is one concern handled in 2+ places? Did grep find an existing owner? Which owner/primitive/tool holds it once? Which duplicate sites were deleted? Does the diff match plan §3c? Consolidate scatter or document genuine separation.

For revision rounds, also grep the document being revised for every other description of the changed symbol/field/mechanism. Reconcile contradictions in the same edit.

Record proof where `check-selfreview-before-codex.sh` reads it. For `codex review`, commit-body lines:

`Self-audit-grep:`
`Self-audit-owner:`

For `codex exec`, create a fresh, <30 min `/tmp/.ew-codex-selfreview-attested` with:

`Checked:`
`Grep:`
`Owner:`

`check-selfreview-before-codex.sh` blocks real Codex calls without proof. `SKIP_SELFAUDIT=1` is audit-logged. Marker lines containing both `<` and `>` are rejected as placeholders; paraphrase such regexes. Write the marker in a separate call before Codex; creating it inside the same compound Bash call does not count.
How to apply: Before a second handler/guard for one concern, stop and name the authority. Before revising mechanism X, run `grep -n <mechanism/symbol name>` on the plan/doc and reconcile all matches.
Ref: PR #355; #1417; 2026-06-28; 2026-07-08.

## RULE: adversarial-depth-not-just-a-lint-pass
Rule: For runtime/concurrency/resource-lifetime diffs, including RT callbacks, format/rate/unit assumptions, buffers, converters, locks, teardown, or XPC, trace one full lifecycle: allocate → write → convert/transform → forward → free. Recheck the current diff against every prior Codex finding category for the file/PR, including RT-safety, use-after-release, overflow, lock-held allocation, format/rate assumption, and teardown race, even at new lines.
How to apply: In the marker or commit body, name the lifecycle traced and finding categories rechecked.
Ref: PR #1418, 2026-07-08.

## RULE: classify-by-producer-not-by-name
Rule: Before assigning behavior to any named case, including telemetry channel, severity, retry policy, or user-facing tone, enumerate every producer and read its physical cause. Never infer from the case name, doc comment, or reviewer speculation. Use `grep -rn "\.<caseName>" Sources/` plus every `classify(`/`from(`/`throw .*<caseName>` site. Before claims about what a type emits or silences, enumerate all emit sites.
How to apply: For enum cases inspect `classify`, `from(_:)`, and every direct `throw`. For emit claims run `grep -n 'Sentry\|Telemetry' <the type>.swift`. Record the producer list before choosing behavior. Pairs with [[absence-claims-need-a-capability-grep]] and [[search-existing-owner-by-capability]].
Ref: #1446 / PR #1460; follow-ups #1458, #1461.

## RULE: search-existing-owner-by-capability
Rule: When §2.5 or §3c asks whether an owner, telemetry path, or handler exists, grep capability synonyms across `Sources/ Tests/`, never only the proposed symbol. Enumerate every caller of the primitive being wrapped. Do not trust issue claims that X lacks Y; paste a negative grep that would have found Y.
How to apply: Search verb+noun plus 2-3 domain synonyms. Telemetry example: `warmup|preload|cold.?start|load.*(ms|duration|started|completed|failed)`. This extends RULE: self-review-and-grep-before-codex and §2.5.
Ref: #1174 PR-5b, 2026-06-23.

## RULE: audit-all-terminal-paths-for-state-reading-telemetry
Rule: When an FSM-terminal capture/read consumes earlier session state, enumerate every path into that terminal and verify: (a) this path sets/freezes the state before transition; (b) for single-winner/latched exits, state is FIRST-WINS under the same latch so later signals cannot decouple it from the winning exit.
How to apply: Grep every terminal transition, including `finishTerminal(.X)`, `deliverRecordingExit(.X)`, and forced transitions, plus every state producer. Mirror the working sibling terminal. Guard state under the same acceptance condition, e.g. `state == .recording && !recordingExitLatched`. This is async-edge-case-enumeration’s Stale + Concurrent case.
Ref: #1174 PR-5b, 2026-06-24.

## RULE: push-discipline-hook
Rule: `.claude/scripts/check-push-discipline.sh`, PreToolUse Bash on `git push`, enforces build freshness, per-branch push-rate budget, and force-push budget. Obey its deny text. Escape hatch `SKIP_PUSH_CHECKS=1 git push ...` appends to `.claude/push-audit.log`.
Ref: `.claude/scripts/check-push-discipline.sh` owns exact thresholds.

## RULE: refactor-tier-discipline
Rule: At REFACTOR/LARGE tier, add no technical debt; if the plan fails, stop and pivot through council. Use existing context7, MCPs, eval harness, and tests rather than reinventing. Agents may scan/research/review only, never change code.
Ref: pre-#154.

## FACT: tier-routing
Classify in the GitHub Issue: `Tier: MEDIUM | Test: required (PostProcessing) | Modules: PostProcessing, Core`.

| Tier | Surface |
|---|---|
| REFACTOR | `Package.swift`, target/module moves, dependency graph, shared protocol reshaping; any module move or multi-conformer protocol change remains REFACTOR regardless of diff size |
| LARGE | Pipeline orchestration, XPC boundaries, app startup, both ASR backends |
| MEDIUM | Audio capture, one ASR backend, Services, permissions, paste path, net-new runtime behavior |
| SMALL | Isolated post-processing, LLM prompt changes, UI layout/copy, config tweaks |

Escalate upward, never downward. Any code edit after validation invalidates that validation. Tier controls test obligations and runtime breadth via RULE: test-obligations and phase3-validation.md FACT: lane-obligations. Plan/council/Codex gates apply except where RULE: council-skip-zero-blast-radius or RULE: council-skip-codex-grounded-review-settled explicitly allows a skip.

## RULE: test-obligations
Rule: In testable Core, PostProcessing, LLM, and Pipeline state/routing: bug fixes require a failing `@Test` before the fix; new logic requires `@Test` alongside or before implementation; threshold/scoring/routing changes require just-above, just-below, and ambiguity tests; subtle refactors require characterization tests first. If no test is added, document why in issue and PR.
How to apply: Use `scripts/xcode-test.sh`; gotchas.md RULE: xcode-test-entrypoint explains why raw `swift test` is not the gate. Runtime-only Audio, XPC, UI, permissions, paste, and hardware are exempt.
Ref: project test baseline.

## RULE: matcher-set-adversarial-tests
Rule: When adding to a skip list, allow list, imperative/preservation set, or matcher where set membership flips routing, test the entry in its non-intended semantic class. A positive plus one happy-path negative is insufficient.
How to apply: Name the intended class, enumerate alternate readings, and add an explicit negative for every meaning-changing alternate. The test harness, not Codex review, must catch it.
Ref: PR #436.

## RULE: parse-structured-input-dont-regex-and-iterate
Rule: Before coding a guard/validator/matcher for structured input, including URL path+query, versions, key=value config, semver, or dates, choose a parser and enumerate the full axes × value-classes matrix. Never ship naive substring/regex matching and discover gaps round by round.
How to apply: List axes, write the complete pass/fail fixture grid, and run a LOCAL adversarial Codex enumeration, `find every input that bypasses each rule across the whole space`, before first push, not through ordinary diff or cloud review. Explicitly document accepted-scope non-authoring inputs such as percent-encoding or backslash normalization.
Ref: PR #1241; [[matcher-set-adversarial-tests]]; [[async-edge-case-enumeration]].

## RULE: async-edge-case-enumeration
Rule: Before coding MEDIUM+, enumerate every async operation against six classes: Interrupted (cancelled mid-flight?), Deleted (data deleted while running?), Mutated (settings/config changed in-flight?), Concurrent (two valid user actions overlap?), Nil (weak reference or optional dependency nil; fail open or closed?), and Stale (cached/snapshot value read after completion?). Record every answer in the Issue or PR. Justify accepted edges; otherwise design the guard before the happy path.

**Closed-set trigger, any tier, overriding MEDIUM+:** Run the full `state × event` matrix only when BOTH hold:

1. The change adds, changes, or reinterprets a closed lifecycle, outcome, or durability contract: state/terminal transition, cancellation/preemption route, session identity, or durable save/cleanup/delete/recovery ordering.
2. Before durable or user-visible commit, two or more ingress classes can affect it: user command, lifecycle/preemption event, timer/task cancellation, or async completion.

ALSO run it when changing the branch that decides whether the heart emits raw text after limb failure, cancellation, or timeout.

Retry, timeout, persistence, status, or fallback mentions alone do not trigger the matrix; use §5/§7-§9 and the six classes. `only`/`exactly`/`never`/`always` are hints, not gates.

Event population: user commands; lifecycle/preemption events including app quit, sleep/wake, device removal, permission revocation, owner deinit, retry timer; and async completions that are absent, duplicate, late, or stale. Enumerate each event’s durable side effects, cleanup, and source-of-truth mutations before terminal state; terminal invariants alone are insufficient.

Run this at plan time from invariant shape. RULE: matrix-review-for-cross-product-surfaces is separate and triggers after 3+ code-diff rounds reveal new cross-product defects.
Ref: async edge baseline; #1408 (`.discarded`/`.noSpeech` → `.discard` deleted a spool `.audioInterrupted` retained; `KernelDictationDriver.swift` `endedWithoutSaveKind`); `docs/audits/2026-07-10-grounding-rule-grounded-review-r1.txt`.

## RULE: lane-detection-match-plan
Rule: Tier controls test scope; lane controls Phase 3. Detect lane with `git diff --name-only <merge-base origin/main HEAD>..HEAD`, never plain `origin/main`. The plan’s lane must match detection.
How to apply: [phase3-validation.md](../knowledge/phase3-validation.md) FACT: lane-obligations solely owns lane globs, obligations, artifacts, and `mixed_pr`. Eval-harness reporting never replaces app Live UAT.
Ref: `scripts/validate-pr.sh`; `detect_lane_from_diff`.

## RULE: definition-of-done
Rule: A change is not done until ALL pass:

1. Xcode release build, `EnviousWispr-Release`.
2. Logic tests via `scripts/xcode-test.sh`; use `--release` for local reproduction or when full Release-suite execution is specifically required.
3. Dev `.app` rebuild + relaunch via `/wispr-rebuild-and-relaunch` (`scripts/build-dev-app.sh`).
4. Each wispr-eyes scope item is VERIFIED or validly SKIPPED per [tools-and-apps.md](./tools-and-apps.md).
5. Council coverage approval on plan and Codex clean on diff, subject only to explicit skip rules.
6. If `/ask-gpt` is used, GPT sign-off after rounds to clean.
7. CI `build-check` green.
8. Close via `gh issue close <number> --comment "reason"`.

How to apply: Checklist numbering is not run order. Codex-clean item 5 precedes items 3-4; then perform one rebuild and Live UAT per RULE: codex-clean-gates-runtime-uat. Wispr-eyes todos use `Fix X in Y (user-visible result Z)` and a fresh list per project. Architecture-sensitive changes also complete [architecture-rules.md § Architecture Definition of Done](./architecture-rules.md) and include Architecture Closeout in the issue closure comment. `.claude/scripts/check-push-discipline.sh` invokes `scripts/check-dependency-direction.sh` on ship-path pushes and blocks violations; SPM cyclic-import compilation is the second line.
Ref: project DoD.

## RULE: grounded-review-mechanics
Rule: After council, before Gate 2, run `codex exec` against the plan with full codebase access. Require PROCEED-AS-PLANNED / PROCEED-WITH-REVISIONS / PIVOT and grep-verified `file:line` corrections. PROCEED-WITH-REVISIONS → revise → rerun until PROCEED-AS-PLANNED. No other verdict reaches Gate 2. Every round verifies prior fixes and scans for new defects.
How to apply: Run `cat <prompt-file> | ~/.claude/bin/codex-5.6-sol exec --sandbox read-only --skip-git-repo-check > docs/audits/<YYYY-MM-DD>-<scope>-grounded-review[-rN].txt 2>&1` via `Bash run_in_background: true`; run from repo root or use an absolute output path. Round 1 contains unrevised plan, both council critiques, and Q1/Q2/Q3/Q4; Q3 is consolidation, Q4 drop-in fixes. Later rounds contain revised plan, prior revision list, and `verify each landed, scan for new defects.` Skip for zero-blast-radius. Flat/rising defect count after 4 rounds is the escalation signal. Cost is $0 via ChatGPT subscription.
Ref: #319; PR-1 #827 3 rounds; #827 epic 7.

## FACT: review-gates-summary
| Gate | Tool | When | Rounds |
|---|---|---|---|
| Council | `~/.local/bin/council ask` | After plan draft | 1, never iterated |
| Grounded Review | `codex exec` | After council, before Gate 2 | Until PROCEED-AS-PLANNED |
| Code-diff review | `codex review` local | After logic tests + self-review, before smoke + Live UAT rebuild | Multiple until clean |
| Cloud review | `chatgpt-codex-connector[bot]` GitHub | After PR open, before auto-merge | Evaluate every finding; fix loop ≤3 per github.md RULE: codex-cloud-review-gate |

Council owns coverage; Grounded Review owns code reality; cloud review independently reviews the open PR. Skip none except RULE: council-skip-zero-blast-radius and RULE: council-skip-codex-grounded-review-settled.

`check-selfreview-before-codex.sh` gates both local Codex passes. `codex review` requires commit `Self-audit-*`; `codex exec` requires fresh `/tmp/.ew-codex-selfreview-attested` with `Checked:`/`Grep:`/`Owner:`. See RULE: self-review-and-grep-before-codex.

## RULE: matrix-review-for-cross-product-surfaces
Rule: If a PR adds methods to a surface crossed with FSM states and code-diff review finds a new substantive defect in each of 3+ rounds, STOP iterating. Ask Codex for the full surface × state matrix; every cell names expected behavior, parity reference, and GAP. Apply only GAP cells naturally belonging to this PR.
How to apply: After 3 consecutive qualifying rounds, prompt with every added/public surface entry, every FSM state, and the parity implementation. Require a markdown matrix, implementation list, and parametric `@Test` freeze test. Use Codex r5 on PR-4b.2 as template.
Ref: PR #856.

## RULE: close-the-window-never-handle-it
Rule: When a plan or invariant says an event CANNOT happen in a window (control disabled, state unreachable), implement the impossibility; never build a handler "to be safe." A handler admits the event, and every admitted event multiplies the state x event surface.
Why: #1386 PR-1 v1 — the plan said "Settings Remove is DISABLED during the transition"; the build instead grew ~120 lines of removal-deferral machinery. 14 of 30+ defects including all 4 cloud P1s trace to that one deviation (25 review rounds). Corollary (same RCA): on the FIRST defect of a class, audit the whole class (all writers/exits/deletes) before patching the shown instance — patched families recur; audited families die.
How to apply: In self-review, any code servicing an event the plan ruled out is a defect even if correct. Full RCA: `docs/postmortems/2026-07-11-1386-pr1-defect-cascade.md`; measured context: workflow-effectiveness.md.
Ref: #1386 PR-1 / PR #1497 (2026-07-11); founder-binding.

## RULE: port-proven-patterns-wholesale
Rule: Port proven state/concurrency patterns whole, including every field and cleanup transition, or state why this instance differs. Never port piecewise across review rounds. If review finds a partial port, stop patching, inventory all fields/transitions, and fix once.
Ref: #1275; council session `ew-1275-warmup-retro-2026-07-02`.

## RULE: route-gaps-not-block-on-them
Rule: In a complex multi-PR rebuild, route each newly discovered gap to this PR / next PR / new follow-up issue instead of treating every gap as off-plan and replanning.
How to apply: Before founder escalation: fix bugs introduced here in this PR; route earlier-PR contract bugs to follow-up issues; route cutover-only validation to the cutover PR with Live UAT. Record routing in the PR description, not only commits.
Ref: PR-4b.2.

## RULE: no-follow-up-tickets-for-the-same-coherent-unit
Rule: Never defer part of the same coherent unit through a follow-up issue or unused seam/mode/flag. Finish it now. Follow-ups are only for a different subsystem, founder decision, or work that cannot be validated with this change.
How to apply: If an abstraction is added for later, wire it now. Split large work into smaller complete slices, never a complete slice plus its deferred remainder. `route-gaps-not-block-on-them` is the narrow exception for unrelated gaps in complex multi-PR rebuilds.
Ref: 2026-06-22 #1151/#1157; CLAUDE.md Rule 14.

## RULE: consult-before-concluding-at-walls
Rule: At dependency conflicts, build failures, apparent impossibility, or a Codex finding implying pivot, consult Codex for code-reality walls or council for design walls before drafting a conclusion or fix direction. Escalate only founder decisions: spend, scope, product, or a genuine fork among sound options.
How to apply: First run the empirical/grounded check, such as resolution attempt, `codex exec`, or generated-code read. Present only options proven workable.
Ref: 2026-05-29 #832.

## RULE: plan-consumers-point-at-the-contract
Rule: One plan section owns each behavioral contract. Plan §5, §7, §9, §11, and §13 identify their local path, caller, projection, or oracle and reference the owner by number; never paraphrase it. Before revising a contract, grep the whole plan for its terms.
How to apply: Use `see §3.2 filter 3`. Keep consumer-specific inputs and observable results. This is the document form of RULE: self-review-and-grep-before-codex and Grounded Review step 5’s single-authority rule.
Ref: #1457.
# Grounding Discipline

## RULE: lookup-ladder
Rule: Walk this ladder in order; stop at the first tier that answers, and name any tier-skip in chat.
| Tier | Source | Use when |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | `.claude/knowledge/*.md` | Question is about an EW decision, gotcha, or past investigation. Pathway map in `CLAUDE.md` § Reference. |
| 1 | Vendor MCP (table in `tech-stack.md` § MCP servers) | Live data OR docs maintained by that vendor's MCP. |
| 2 | `context7` (`resolve-library-id` → `query-docs`, `researchMode: true` on retry) | Public framework / library / SDK / CLI docs. |
| 3 | Checkout grep (`.build/checkouts/<dep>/`, `Package.resolved` for SHA, `node_modules/<dep>/`) | Pinned versions, fork-specific patches (`saurabhav88/FluidAudio` `e7948e1a`), undocumented internals. |
| 4 | WebFetch / WebSearch | Tiers 0–3 returned nothing. Cite the URL. |
| 5 | Ask Saurabh | Product / business / preference only. Never for technical lookups. |
Why: Tier-skips cause drift.
Ref: 2026-05-03 — context7 covers public docs; a custom RAG over them wasn't the fit then (re-evaluate if corpus/tooling grows). Retrieval over our OWN knowledge base is a separate, still-open question.

## RULE: tier-skip-must-be-named
Rule: Name every Tier 1 or Tier 2 skip in chat, then stop and use the right tier if the skip was accidental.
Why: Naming forces a sanity check and creates a grep-able audit trail.
Ref: 2026-05-03.

## RULE: pinned-fork-grep-only
Rule: Grep `.build/checkouts/FluidAudio/` for `saurabhav88/FluidAudio` and any fork pinned off upstream `main`; do not trust upstream docs.
Why: Upstream docs describe code we're not running because we pinned the fork for its patches.
Ref: 2026-05-03.

## RULE: absence-claims-need-a-capability-grep
Rule: In a plan, council prompt, Codex prompt, or chat, any “we have no X,” “X does not exist,” or “no consumers” claim needs a pasted capability search, not a search for the expected name. Absence of memory is not evidence. Negative-claim twin of [[search-existing-owner-by-capability]]. This is the one home for the command shape; `TEMPLATE.md` §2.5 item 5 references it and must not restate it.

```
$ grep -rln "<syn1>\|<syn2>" Sources/ Tests/ workers/ website/ .github/ scripts/ .claude/ docs/ AGENTS.md CLAUDE.md | sort
```

- Name the lane and justify every excluded root. These roots are defaults, not a population. Tooling searches include `.claude/` and `docs/`; omitting them hides the 26 validators in `.claude/scripts/`.
- A directory list never reaches a root-level FILE. `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` are gitignored and live at the repo root, so `git grep` and every directory-scoped sweep skip both. #1459's retirement sweep scoped itself to `scripts/ Sources/ Tests/ .github/` plus `.claude/` and declared the tracked tree clean; five references to the retired launcher were still sitting in `AGENTS.md`, the file that configures the Codex reviewer (2026-07-09).
- `| sort` is mandatory. Paste every hit verbatim and uncurated; put interpretation below the block.
- Synonyms: alerting → `alert|threshold|THRESHOLDS|notify|webhook`; scheduling → `cron|schedule|workflow_dispatch`; telemetry → `capture|emit|track|event`.
- A hit kills the claim only when it implements the capability. Classify incidental matches before concluding.

| Class | Required proof |
|---|---|
| **Closed-world:** a finite authority defines all members, such as an enum, state switch, dispatcher, or conformer set. | Cite the authority (`file:line`), paste the structural enumeration, state expected and observed counts, and account for every member. Do not search for the counterexample you expect. |
| **Open-world:** no finite authority defines all members, such as alerting infrastructure, external consumers, vendor rules, dashboards, or unversioned configuration. | Search every justified source and write “no implementation found in `<sources>`,” never “X does not exist.” Do not invent an authority. |

`grep -rn "editUI" Sources/` is not closed-world proof because it neither enumerates an authority nor accounts for its members. Closed-set lifecycle invariants follow `workflow-process.md` RULE: async-edge-case-enumeration, which owns the trigger, matrix, and tier scope.

Ref: #1408, #1446, #1453; `docs/audits/2026-07-10-grounding-rule-grounded-review-r1.txt`.

## RULE: read-the-fact-not-the-first-n-lines
Rule: When the non-blocking pre-read hook or pathway map names a knowledge file, LOOK UP the fact needed for the decision — `grep -n "<capability>"` the file, then read the matching `## FACT:` / `## RULE:` entry. Opening the first lines is not a lookup.
Why: #1446 read `analytics-operations.md` in full and later contradicted `FACT: product-health-worker`. The file was present in context; the failure was treating retrieval as compliance and making a negative claim without the required capability grep. The non-blocking hook now resurfaces matching headings and records read/unread status, but the §2.5 grounding brief owns proof.
How to apply: `grep -n "^## FACT:\|^## RULE:" <file>` to see the file's index, then read the entries that match your scope. Cheaper than a full read and it cannot miss a fact that lives at line 150. If two knowledge files disagree, that is a DRY defect — fix it in the same session (one fact, one home; the other file gets a cross-link), do not silently pick the one you read first.
Ref: #1446 (2026-07-09); the stale duplicate lived in `sentry-operations.md` FACT: live-sentry-alert-rules, corrected the same day.

## RULE: grep-the-dependency-for-lifecycle-before-hand-rolling
Rule: Before designing ANY bespoke lifecycle/state-machine/manager/coalescing/retry infrastructure around a dependency (model load/unload, connection pool, session lifecycle, single-flight, cache), FIRST grep that dependency's `.build/checkouts/<dep>/` for an existing manager/state type it already ships (`*Manager`, `*State`, `*Coordinator`, `ensure*`, `coalesc`, `enum .*State`) — and check whether the object you already hold exposes it. Adopt-vs-invent is a DESIGN-TIME decision, not a post-hoc discovery.
Why: #1276 Step 1 designed a bespoke 9-field load-state machine + single-flight through 5 Codex rounds + council + an adversarial robustness pass — then the founder asked "are we reinventing the wheel?" and a 10-minute source grep found the toolkit already ships `ModelState` (a canonical lifecycle enum, live on every `WhisperKit` instance) AND `ModelManager` (download→load→unload state machine WITH concurrency coalescing + retry). ~Half the plan was reinvention. The review rounds validated invariants but never asked "does the library already do this."
How to apply: Extends [[search-existing-owner-by-capability]] (which greps OUR `Sources/`) to the DEPENDENCY's checkout. The §2.5 grounding brief for any infra plan must include a "did I grep the library for an existing lifecycle/manager?" line with the negative-or-positive grep pasted. Genuinely-ours policy (EW-specific latency tricks, fail-open budgets, use-leases) is what survives the cross-reference — build a thin layer of THAT on the library's machinery, not a parallel machine.
Ref: #1276 (2026-07-03); inventory `docs/feature-requests/issue-1276-step1-buy-vs-build-inventory.md`; whisperkit-research.md FACT: toolkit ships its OWN model-lifecycle machinery.

## RULE: never-hand-a-reviewer-an-unchecked-premise
Rule: A council or Codex prompt gives every asserted fact the plan’s evidence burden. Verify each premise or label it “not verified” in the prompt. Reviewer prompts are plan text.

How to apply: Check every negative, historical, causal, and quantitative claim before sending it. Negative claims follow [[absence-claims-need-a-capability-grep]]. Council has no repo access; agreement with an unchecked premise is not validation.

A claim about a PRIOR REVIEW's contents is a premise. Grep the audit file for the finding; never read its tail through a fixed line window. A `codex exec` transcript repeats the final block, so a window can straddle two copies and silently truncate the findings list. #1462: told the reviewer it had shipped no fix for a section when it had, after reading a truncated Q4 of an 866 KB audit — then miscounted the same list twice more (6, then 8; it was 9).

Ref: #1446, #1453, #1457, #1462.

# Engineering Conventions
Production-breaking rules live in [gotchas.md](gotchas.md); workflow policy lives in [`.claude/rules/workflow-process.md`](../rules/workflow-process.md).
## RULE: xcode-build-engine-cli
Rule: Build EnviousWispr with Xcode's build engine driven from the CLI via the committed Tuist manifests (`Project.swift` / `Tuist.swift`). Do not commit the generated `.xcodeproj` / `.xcworkspace`, and do not treat raw `swift build` / `swift test` as the build or test path for the app, XPC services, release archive, or repo test gate.
Why: The SwiftPM-only path could not place Core ML / SwiftPM resources where the generated `Bundle.module` accessor resolves them in a signed `.app` (#913). Xcode owns app + 2 XPC + test + resource assembly; Tuist keeps it reproducible and CLI-only with no committed project files. (`swift build` of the package products still compiles as an informal quick sanity check, but it is not the canonical build and never produces the shippable bundle.)
How to apply: Dev bundle → `scripts/build-dev-app.sh` (Tuist generate → xcodebuild `EnviousWispr-Dev`/`Dev` config → `build/EnviousWispr Local.app`); logic tests → `scripts/xcode-test.sh`; release DMG → `scripts/build-release-dmg.sh <version>`. Kill old dev processes by `EnviousWispr Local.app` executable path (ALL dev instances — one dev EW at a time, founder 2026-07-01), never `pkill -x EnviousWispr` (dev and prod share the `EnviousWispr` executable name). Enforced (nudge, not a block): `.claude/scripts/check-swift-build-gate.sh` (PreToolUse Bash) fires on raw `swift test` or `swift build -c release`/`--configuration release` and points at the real script; plain `swift build` (debug, with or without `--target`) stays silent as the allowed sanity check. The real wall is downstream: `check-push-discipline.sh` keys push freshness to the Xcode-engine artifact's mtime, so a raw swift build/test can never silently satisfy Definition of Done even if the nudge is ignored.
Ref: [xcode-build-tooling.md](xcode-build-tooling.md); [gotchas.md](gotchas.md) RULE: xcode-test-entrypoint; 2026-07-11 (this session caught itself using `swift build -c release` as the release gate, no hook existed).
## FACT: commit-style
Conventional commits: `type(scope): message`, squash-merged. Types: `feat` / `fix` / `refactor` / `docs` / `chore` / `test` / `perf`. Scopes: `asr` / `audio` / `ui` / `llm` / `pipeline` / `settings` / `hotkey` / `vad` / `build` / `user` / `release`. Example: `feat(hotkey): chord default for fresh installs`.
## RULE: di-narrow-homes
Rule: Inject the narrow `@Environment` home a view needs (e.g. `LiveRecordingState`); never add a broad cross-domain state object and read it as `<object>.<domain>...`.
Why: That god-object pattern is what made AppState the deletion target of epic #763.
How to apply: Settings changes route through `PipelineSettingsSync.handleSettingChanged()`; AppState is freeze-test blocked from reintroduction; full decision tree in [state-ownership.md](state-ownership.md).
Ref: epic #763.
## RULE: settings-persistence
Rule: Persist non-sensitive settings via `UserDefaults.standard` with `didSet`; persist API keys via `KeychainManager` (Apple Keychain for release `com.enviouswispr.app`, file storage at `~/.enviouswispr-keys/` for DEBUG/dev/unrecognized bundles, failing closed).
Why: Failing closed means UAT and beta builds never touch the production Keychain.
Ref: [gotchas.md](gotchas.md) RULE: keychain-two-backends.
## FACT: view-patterns
- Settings tab body: `SettingsContentView { ... }` (branded ScrollView wrapper in `SettingsComponents.swift`; replaced `Form { }.formStyle(.grouped)`).
- Main window: one unified window — `UnifiedWindowView` (`SettingsView.swift`), a `NavigationStack` + custom `HStack` whose sidebar is a `ScrollView` of button rows (NOT a `NavigationSplitView`/`List` — that was replaced for macOS-26 sizing; the sidebar renders as `AXButton`s, uat-testing.md #1296) driven by the `SettingsSection` enum (History + every settings tab); onboarding is a separate `Window(id: "onboarding")`.
- Async on-appear work: `.task { }`.
## FACT: release-versioning
Semver tags with `v` prefix (`v1.2.3`) trigger release CI; local builds carry `-local` suffix (`0.0.0-local`). Full release flow: [github.md](github.md) RULE: release-workflow and [distribution.md](distribution.md). Changelog: `/wispr-generate-changelog`.
## RULE: brand-visual-system
Rule: Use the brand system at `.claude/skills/brand-guide/SKILL.md` for all visual artifacts (HTML mockups, diagrams, landing pages) — tokens, fonts, gradients.
Why: It is the SoT; ad-hoc choices drift the brand.
How to apply: macOS settings use system-ui fonts plus brand accent purple (`#7c3aed`) instead of the default blue.
Ref: `.claude/skills/brand-guide/SKILL.md`.
## RULE: feature-request-docs
Rule: Track feature specs as GitHub Issues; save plans at `docs/feature-requests/issue-<N>-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<slug>.md` (no-issue plans: `plan-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<slug>.md`); all plans outside the zero-blast-radius exception use `TEMPLATE.md` with §4-9 mandatory regardless of tier.
Why: Bias toward SMALL tier is a documented failure mode; legacy files stay to avoid history churn.
How to apply: Commit scope matches the primary code area (e.g. `feat(hotkey):`). New naming applies from 2026-04-14 forward.
Ref: [workflow-process.md](../rules/workflow-process.md) RULE: ten-step-shape (step 3).
## RULE: logging-convention
Rule: Use `AppLogger.shared` actor (always `await` at call site) via `log(_:level:category:)`; never log secrets.
Why: Single actor avoids duplicate sink setup; secrets in logs leak in CI artifacts.
How to apply: Levels `.verbose` / `.debug` / `.info`; categories `"Pipeline"` / `"LLM"` / `"Audio"` / `"PipelineTiming"`; the ENTIRE log pipeline (OSLog + file sink) is `#if DEBUG`-only — release builds are dead code, no output; in DEBUG, OSLog receives every call and the file sink at `~/Library/Logs/EnviousWispr/app.log` (5×10MB rotation) is active only when debug mode is enabled; `CORRECTION_DEBUG` category traces RAW ASR → WordCorrector → Filler Removal → LLM Polish per utterance.
Ref: PR #85.
## RULE: xpc-error-sanitize
Rule: Never call `safeReply(error as NSError)` in `ASRServiceHandler` (the sole remaining XPC service handler after #1543 collapsed audio capture in-process); always pass error paths through `XPCErrorSanitizer.sanitizeForXPC(error)` with the `// XPC error sanitization boundary.` anchor comment.
Why: Unsanitized NSError userInfo can SIGABRT the helper during XPC encode.
How to apply: `scripts/check-xpc-error-hygiene.sh` enforces this in CI.
Ref: issue #297.
## FACT: runtime-cross-refs
Runtime behavior, types, data flow: [pipeline-mechanics.md](pipeline-mechanics.md) (text processing, streaming, audio capture, paste cascade) and [architecture.md](architecture.md) (state machines, modules, window architecture).
## RULE: no-dotclaude-from-shipped-code
Rule: Never reference `.claude/` from shipped files — `Sources/**`, `Tests/**`, `Package.swift`, `.github/workflows/**` (comments / docs / runtime strings), `website/src/**`, `website/public/**`, `workers/**` (prompts shipped to the cloud); inline the salient sentence, move load-bearing content to `docs/` and link there, or delete the cross-ref if it is just attribution.
Why: Ship-path `.claude/` links break on GitHub, fresh clones, and cloud worker prompts.
How to apply: Allowed exceptions are runtime calls to `~/.claude/bin/get-key`, CI path-allowlist regex patterns, and content explicitly about the `.claude/` folder (e.g. blog posts).
Ref: PR #717 (2026-05-10).
